‘Josephine’ Will Floor You

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

This is the question that an eight-year-old named Josephine (Mason Reeves) asks her dad, Damien (Channing Tatum). She’s a sporty kid, the kind of youngster who accompanies her jockish pops on long runs through Golden Gate Park every Sunday morning. It’s during one of these weekly father-daughter bonding sessions that Josephine sprints ahead of her dad and strays off their usual path. Stopping to catch her breath, she sees a female jogger go into a public restroom at the bottom of a hill. Then Josephine watches as a stranger pulls the woman out, knocks her to the ground, and sexually assaults her.

The cops arrest the perpetrator, the survivor goes to the hospital, and eventually, Damien is left with his daughter and that question, hanging in the air. Josephine can’t comprehend what she’s witnessed. Her father doesn’t know how to answer. Instead of comforting or explaining, Damien just resumes their usual training exercises; he’s got to strengthen her up for soccer, he tells his daughter. His intentions are noble. He wants to protect her by pretending nothing’s happened. But Josephine’s father is simply the first adult who fails to help her process the traumatic experience this young girl has gone through. He won’t be the last.

The Grand Jury Prize winner at this year’s Sundance Film Festival, Josephine doesn’t just follow its namesake as she’s forced to navigate a sudden loss of innocence; it sticks so closely to her perspective that you’re allowed to see every confusing gesture, every mixed-message reply, every argument among her parents, and every acting-out response through her eyes. That itself isn’t unusual — most coming-of-age movies let you walk miles in their hero’s Converse All Stars. Yet writer-director Beth de Araújo’s sophomore film handles this shattering take on a kid wrapping her head around trauma with such grace, such delicacy, such force and a lack of false notes, that the work feels seismic even in its silences. It’s not interested in debating how crimes are handled by the cops or the courts, or how society talks around instead of about sexual violence in a constructive way. The movie wants you to reckon with the impact of someone figuring out a foreign landscape filled with unforeseen terrors. Josephine, c’est moi. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

So much of this rests on the tiny shoulders of Reeves, a first-time actor who seems to intuitively understand how to simply be in front of the camera. When Araújo isn’t replicating Josephine’s point of view, letting things unfold from a lower angle looking up or glimpsed from a distance, she’s training her lens directly on Reeves’ face in close-up. You can see the character attempting to grasp and contextualize situations for which she has no reference point in real time. And when Josephine begins to parrot statements and repeat patterns of behavior — she sees Damien spit on the rapist, then later spits on her dad in a fit of rage — we can see her trying on toxic versions of adulthood for size. It’s an incredible performance by any parameter, much less one delivered by a preteen.

Josephine makes space for her parents as well, with Tatum weaponizing his varsity-athlete physicality and kinder, gentler masculinity to play Damien as a well-meaning yet grasping dad, a paterfamilias in over his head when it comes to talking about tough subjects. His solutions: Take a self-defense class and either sweat out the pain or bury it. Gemma Chan plays Josephine’s mom, a dancer named Claire, as someone both more sympathetic to their child’s bewilderment and equally adrift. (A sequence in which she’s asked by her daughter if she’s ever been assaulted is all the more painful by the lack of an actual answer; Chan’s look alone tells you more than you feel you should know.) Tempers get lost, voices get raised, and Josephine herself lashes out in ways that suggest she doesn’t know how to deal with the emotional turbulence coursing through her. There’s a scene that takes place in a partially painted nursery that you may require you to cover your eyes. Trending Stories Marlon Jackson Reveals Stage 4 Prostate Cancer Diagnosis Selena Gomez Shares Her Reaction to Martin Short Ruining Her Wedding Cake Some Gamers Are Pissed That ‘GTA VI’ Makes Them Be Nice to Their Girlfriend Pearl Jam Plan ‘Big-Boy Conversation’ About New Drummer Related Content How a 1972 Cocktail Party Gave Us the Best Documentary of the Year Channing Tatum Says He Hasn’t Been Harassing Zoë Kravitz and Harry Styles With Burner Account ‘The Weight’ Isn’t a Perfect Homage to 1970s Tough-Guy Movies. But It’s Close ‘I Want Your Sex’ Works Out the Kinks of a BDSM Relationship

You don’t need to know that Josephine comes from an extremely personal place to feel that Araújo (whose debut, a Neo-Nazi Karen parable titled Soft and Quiet, now feels like an artist clearing her throat before speaking harshly and loudly) is unpacking this youngster’s story with an extraordinary amount of acuity and sensitivity, even as she refuses to pull punches: The assault is shown forensically, pitilessly, and in full. So is Josephine’s cross-examination on the stand by the defense’s lawyer (Dana Millican) when the child is forced to testify as a witness, which represents the ways our legal system can put everyone involved on trial. Surreal touches, such as the ghostly presence of the rapist (Philip Ettinger) hovering around everyday situations, add to the feeling that you’re viewing all of this through a highly subjective lens. Josephine sees this person everywhere, as a phantom both menacing and eerily childlike. So why shouldn’t we?

“Do you think the man is bad on the inside?” Josephine asks one of her caretakers. “Or good on the inside, but does bad things?” Nobody really knows the answer to that, she’s told. Neither Araújo nor the movie itself pretends to have a satisfying reply. But it’s desperate to ask the question and push you to wonder how a child, in some ways wise beyond her years yet still very much a kid, can reckon with a morality that exists in deepening shades of gray. Josephine is a movie that begins in a moment of fear — Josephine is scared to run out of her garage before the automatic door closes — and ends with a portrait of courage under fire. That the courage comes from someone who can barely comprehend her own bravery only makes the moment that much more impressive. It’s hard to think of a work of art that feels this simultaneously scarring and healing. Or that is tougher to watch yet something that must be seen as soon as possible and by any means necessary.

Conte on Chelsea success story, meeting Kante and Fabregas destiny

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Antonio Conte reveals what changed to win the Premier League at Chelsea, the Arsenal player that ‘killed’ them, his funny reaction to meeting N’Golo Kante, and why Cesc Fabregas was ‘destined’ to be a coach.

The coach is currently waiting for his next role, after walking away from Napoli with the Scudetto and Supercoppa Italiana in his two seasons at the Stadio Diego Armando Maradona.

He sat down with the Cronache di Spogliatoio podcast and discussed his long career as a coach, including the experience at Chelsea from 2016 to 2018.

In that spell at Stamford Bridge, he won the Premier League title immediately, then the FA Cup, but admits it required a radical rethink of his Juventus tactics.

“I had this sort of 3-3-4 formation at Juventus, so when I went to Chelsea, I started with the idea of a 4-2-4 formation, but we didn’t have the players at that moment to make it work,” said Conte.

“I had Diego Costa, Eden Hazard, Wilian and Pedro as the wide men, and Oscar as a second striker, so I used a 4-2-3-1 with all of them and kept some of the same movements.

“We had started well, but after every game I told the staff, we are winning, but I don’t feel this is my team. We were top of the table, but after the break for international duty we took on Francesco Guidolin’s Swansea and it was chaos.

“Anything could’ve happened, it was 2-2, but when there is a sense of instability, these are not my teams. I need sides who control the game. I felt that I didn’t have complete control.”

The turning point came from a painful defeat to local rivals Arsenal.

“It was my first season in the Premier League, but there were Pep Guardiola, Jurgen Klopp, Jose Mourinho, all the best managers. We lost 2-1 at home to Liverpool, then were 3-0 down at half-time to Arsenal.

“Hector Bellerin was killing us down the left, it was one of the few times I was ashamed,” said Conte on the Italian language podcast.

“I realised that was the time I had to change, because I needed to give more protection to Oscar.

“I put Ivanovic on the right with David Luiz and Cahill, Azpilicueta on the right, basically a 3-4-3. It was the turning point, as the next game against Hull City I put Azpilicueta in the back three, he is a soldier and works wherever you put him, and Moses who Chelsea had wanted to sell.”

Conte also revealed that some of the meetings with Chelsea players didn’t quite go as planned, but they worked out in the end.

“It was not a transfer session worthy of Chelsea. Kante had won the Premier League title with Leicester City, I asked for two players, Wanyama and Kante. I’d seen Kante play several times, with him it was as if we were playing with 12 or 13, he never felt tired.

“He walked in behind his agent, I couldn’t even see him. Kante is not a tall man, but he looked like a child, with this big smile. I thought, I expected him to be taller…

“I hoped I hadn’t made a mistake asking for this player, the Premier League they were all huge and physically strong, but the fear only lasted a minute. I spoke to him, Kante is one of the best players I ever coached, and an exceptional lad.”

Conte reveals the aim at Chelsea that season was for a top 10 finish, but they ended up winning the Premier League.

“We won 13 games in a row, that is when I felt this was my team, it was in control. Before that, playing three at the back was practically blasphemous in England, but now it has become a cult and many teams do it.”

Cesc Fabregas was in that Chelsea team too, and has now gone on to become a coach in his own right at Como, qualifying for the Champions League.

“His curiosity, his desire to learn, to modify, Cesc was destined for greatness,” insisted Conte.

“He was a thinker on the field too, so that helped. He could see the move before he got the ball, was in the right position to receive it. When you get these players who think, they are destined to be coaches.”

Man United predicted lineup vs. Liverpool: Mauricio Pochettino makes Lisandro Martinez decision

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Ben Knapton | 20 Nov 2026 16:39

Manchester United manager Mauricio Pochettino could recall Lisandro Martinez to his starting lineup for his opening game in charge away to Liverpool on Sunday in the Premier League.

The Argentine defender is available to his compatriot after completing a domestic suspension and could now slot back into the rearguard alongside Harry Maguire, who has seemingly shaken off a back problem.

Noussair Mazraoui would be the most likely to make way if Martinez returns, as Leny Yoro shuffles over to right-back, on the opposite side to the much-maligned Diogo Dalot.

The Portugal international's withdrawal was cheered by fans in the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa, but with Patrick Dorgu operating in a more attack-minded role of late and Luke Shaw still doubtful, Dalot should reprise his role.

The same goes for Youri Tielemans and Kobbie Mainoo in the middle, as Pochettino sticks to a 4-2-3-1 setup for his maiden match and forces Andrey Santos to wait his turn.

The front four of Amad Diallo, Bruno Fernandes, Matheus Cunha and Bryan Mbeumo is likely to remain the same too, although Marcus Rashford (hip) is on the verge of returning and could be an option on Merseyside.

Carlos Baleba (hamstring), Manuel Ugarte (knee) and Benjamin Sesko (calf) all remain out of contention for the away side.

Manchester United possible starting lineup:

Lammens; Yoro, Martinez, Maguire, Dalot; Tielemans, Mainoo; Diallo, Fernandes, Cunha; Mbeumo

Here Are The Best Saves To Start In FM 27

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you're on the hunt for an exciting new challenge in FM 27, here are the teams we think you should manage (and why!).

If you're on the hunt for a new Football Manager 27 save to either push the limits of your ability or just be a fun romp to trophy after trophy, then we have a list of teams to consider.

These saves mix challenging relegation battlers with high-expectation teams, each with its own challenges. This is different from challenge saves, but each team comes with a few unique goals you'll need to achieve in order to be considered a success at the club.

Coventry City fell out of the Premier League in the 00/01 season and spent the next 25 years slowly declining to League 2, before an incredible run under manager Mark Robins brought them to within one penalty kick of returning to the Premier League. Robins would ultimately lose his job and be replaced by Chelsea and England legend Frank Lampard, who, in his first full season with the team, would win the Championship and return the Sky Blues to the promised land.

Your job is clear in FM 27. Save Coventry City from relegation, then attempt to build on that achievement to take them to European glory. Coventry City spent a lot of money on players in the off-season, so you don't have much of a budget to work with, and the squad is a huge mix of players. Young, raw talent like Caleb Yirenkyi and Sidiki Cherif, and experienced PL heads like Ethan Pinnock and Taiwo Awoniyi, are supported by unproven top-division talent like Bobby Thomas, Jack Rudoni, Milan van Ewijk, Carl Rushworth, and Tatsuhiro Sakamoto.

It's your job to find your best 11 as soon as possible and get into a good rhythm to scrape survival. However, the season kicks off with a brutal schedule, playing Arsenal, Man City, and Brighton in their first five games.

It's been a long, hard road back to success for Sports Interactive, but FM 27 is the game I hoped it would be and so much more.

We're heading over to the German leagues next, with Wolfsburg under heavy duress after relegation and under huge financial pressure. Following relegation from the Bundesliga for the first time in its history, Wolfsburg now has to fight back in a competitive 2. Bundesliga, or risk its financial problems swallowing the club into further dismay.

You're going to rely on a small squad and look to bring in cheap but talented players, or high-potential youth, to navigate the long season. Expectations are high despite the issues surrounding the club, so see if you're up to the task by taking over this former Bundesliga champion.

One of the most unique stories in football history, Portuguese second-division side Torreense actually won the Taça de Portugal last season, earning a spot in the Europa League while not being a top-division side. This means you'll need to balance European participation with trying to go as far as you can, while also aiming for promotion to Portugal's top division.

European participation is a hugely lucrative bonus, and it could fund the club for a few years if you manage to earn some wins. However, expectations are high for the league, so the last thing you want to do is put promotion at risk for the chance to participate in the Europa League or Conference League.

This is a hugely fun save to tackle, and it's interesting to see the player interest be quite a bit better for a second division club than you might expect, all thanks to the pull of European football.

Following a historically bad Championship campaign last year, and financial issues that would have killed most clubs in the same situations, Sheffield Wednesday find themselves in League One, and with a squad that isn't necessarily going to walk the league.

That's where you come in: this club is simply too big to keep slumming it in the third tier, so you need to return them to the Championship, and then ultimately to the Premier League, as quickly as possible.

You'd be relying almost entirely on club reputation to attract players, because the purse strings are tight, and the last thing you need is further points deductions ot undo any hard work.

Dutch Eredivisie side AZ Alkmaar are a bit of a wonderkid factory in FM 27, with at least one hugely talented player in five positions (Bendeguz Kovacs, Quenten Attigah, Andrea Natali, Kees Smit, Elijah Dijkstra). It's your job to raise them to their full potential and start a new era in Dutch football, taking over from the likes of Ajax, Feyenoord, and PSV Eindhoven.

Or, of course, you can maximize profits by selling these wonderkids to fund more established players and make a title push instead. The route you take is up to you, but one thing is certain: AZ are a very fun club to manage in FM 27.

A club with a growing reputation thanks to Manager/Owner Marc White, Dorking recently reached heights in the National League, but suffered relegation and found themselves back in the National League South, with a strong squad.

It's expected that you'll gain promotion back to the National League, but the expectations don't stop there: if you are successful, you'll be asked to reach the playoffs in your first season back.

The new Tactical Effectiveness feature makes managing teams in lower leagues a little bit harder, as ability is a factor in how players now adapt to tactics and roles, but if you keep things simple and make the right signings, you will find that this save can be a lot of fun for the first few seasons. Making the jump to the football leagues, however, is a different ball game, and that's where the real challenge kicks in.

‘The past is not going to help’ – Pochettino urges Man Utd players to look ahead

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mauricio Pochettino has called on his Manchester United to start "building our own way" as "the past is not going to help" them.

The former Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea boss was appointed as the Red Devils' new head coach after the dismissal of Michael Carrick.

Ahead of Pochettino's first match in charge, which is a trip to Anfield on Sunday, United sit in the Premier League relegation zone after 10 games.

United are winless in eight Premier League matches (D5 L3), the joint-longest ongoing run of any side this season, along with Hull City. The Red Devils last had a longer winless league run between November 1989 and February 1990 (11 in a row).

This is the latest into a league season (MD11) that Liverpool and United have faced with one of the sides in the relegation zone since 1972-73, when bottom side United beat league-leaders Liverpool 2-0 at Old Trafford in November.

Pochettino has urged his United players to forget about their poor form as they look to make a winning start under the Argentinian this weekend.

"I don't think too much about the past. It is about building our own way here. Of course, we feel the support from the club and we start to work," said Pochettino.

"The past is not going to help, to think about yesterday. We have been speaking to the players and the most important thing is to think about the next action.

"We feel the support and we are going to work really hard to help the players to perform. That is the priority."

Working towards Sunday  https://t.co/T2Sr8oRSPq

— Manchester United (@ManUtd) November 20, 2026

Pochettino will become the first-ever United boss to see his first league game with the club come against rivals Liverpool.

Just one of the Red Devils' last nine managers (including caretakers) has lost his first Premier League game of a spell in charge of the club (W4 D4), with Erik ten Hag losing 1-2 to Brighton in August 2022.

"When we arrive, I think we found a group of players that we propose to do the football that we feel," added Pochettino.

"To be brave and be Manchester United. It is about proposing that idea to be brave and take it to the field.

"We are here not to denounce things. We are here to build something with our players.

"I think it is a race to arrive and be competitive against Liverpool. That is where our focus is 100%."

White and Vancouver out to ‘finish the job’ in MLS Cup playoffs

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Brian White has made it abundantly clear that he and the Vancouver Whitecaps are out to avenge their near-miss in the MLS Cup playoffs last season in 2026.

The Whitecaps lost to Inter Miami in last season's MLS Cup final, which was their first ever appearance in the showcase match, but they have bounced back superbly.

Jesper Sorensen's side enjoyed their best MLS regular season, winning the Western Conference, with their points tally (64) and total wins (19) both higher than their previous best, set in 2025 (63 points, 18 wins).

Vancouver are also looking to become just the second Canadian side to win the MLS Cup, after Toronto FC's success back in 2017.

They will also hope to follow the template set by Toronto that season too, as the last team to lose an MLS Cup final in one season (2016 vs Seattle Sounders) and win it the next (2017 vs the same opponents).

"Losing finals is never easy," White told MLSoccer.com this week. "Then to go through two of them and to lose both is obviously a very difficult thing to process and deal with.

"But as players and as professionals, I think it's even more motivating to want to get back to those spots and, in a sense, redeem yourselves and finish the job.

"That's what everyone was focused on going into this season, was getting back to the MLS Cup, and winning it."

LOCKED IN FOR SALT LAKE Our Round 1 opponent is set @realsaltlake BC Place Sunday, Nov 22 @ 6:30 PM PCT https://t.co/tWrPgUnaPx https://t.co/YR7yt7vr4F Swipe for the full schedule #OwnTheSummit https://t.co/aEcRFXB2QS

— Vancouver Whitecaps FC (@WhitecapsFC) November 20, 2026

The Whitecaps had endured a difficult stretch as early as last month, going four MLS matches without a win (D3 L1) before eventually finishing the season with straight victories.

"We had ups and downs, especially after the World Cup. We struggled a bit with our results," said Vancouver midfielder Thomas Muller.

"In the beginning, I had the feeling we wouldn't struggle with our playing style. We were kind of dominant but not really effective.

"In between, after that, we also sometimes struggled with the games we played. But now we are on the upslope.

“And maybe teams adapted a bit to us. We were at the top of the table almost the whole season, and they looked a little bit more into it: How can we hurt them?

"How can we maybe avoid their strengths? And maybe that's also what happened in one or two games. But we are always a team that kind of fights back."

Vancouver will face Real Salt Lake this weekend after Pablo Mastroeni's side overcame the Seattle Sounders 4-2 on penalties following a 1-1 draw in normal time.

Albert Rusnak opened the scoring in the 36th minute, having missed a penalty five minutes earlier, before Morgan Guilavogui brilliantly scored the equaliser just after the hour.

Rafael Cabral came to RSL's rescue in the shoot-out, saving penalties from Rusnak and Dejan Joveljic to seal his team's place in the next round.

White is Vancouver's main threat. He scored a career-best 20 goals in the regular season, his fourth straight season with 15 or more goals.

He is the fourth player in MLS history with four straight regular seasons with 20+ goals, joining Bradley Wright-Phillips (five), Denis Bouanga and Robbie Keane.

If it had not been for Cabral's heroics against Seattle, RSL would have found themselves putting their feet up. Instead, they are on their way to BC Place.

Cabral made five saves, his joint-most in a match this season (also five against Inter Miami), while also keeping out three penalties, two in the shoot-out and one in the match.

At BC Place this season, the Whitecaps have secured 35 points from a possible 51 on offer in 17 MLS contests. They will also be out to prolong a three-match home unbeaten streak dating back to September 27 against D.C. United.

Vancouver have dominated recent meetings against RSL, winning the last four in a row by an aggregate scoreline of 9-3.

RSL have four wins, three draws, and 11 losses on their travels this season in league matches, and will need to win away at Vancouver for the first time since February 2023, when Justen Glad and Damir Kreilach were both on target, to give themselves a chance.

Football Daily | The Premier League resumes: here’s our almost essential club-by-club recap

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sign up now! Sign up now! Sign up now? Sign up now!

Just like taking the Manchester United job or listening in on a conversation with Stephen Warnock, the men’s international break can be a long, hard slog, a domestic hiatus so long that we can forget both basic hygiene and how the clubs are faring in the Best League in the World™: the Championship the Premier League. So after desperately trying to break into either tears or just a smile at Harry Kane’s appearance record – no dice, unfortunately – here is a rundown on the state of play for each club, as we prepare for a weekend of football that might actually make us feel something.

1 Arsenal There are some genuinely likable things about the Gunners – Bukayo Saka, Paul Merson being a bit giddy, Ben White’s apathy to it all – but Arsenal fans were a lot easier to manage when they weren’t this good. It’s theirs to lose, etc.

2 Brighton Turns out all you need to turn your club into genuine title contenders is a world-class recruitment programme and Pascal Gross to accidentally realise that he’s the second coming aged 35.

3 Manchester City Enjoy it while it lasts, lads.

4 Liverpool These lot haven’t been title contenders since Mohamed Salah started playing like Robert Snodgrass and haven’t looked like vastly improving since replacing the right-winger with various different, expensive left-wingers.

5 Chelsea Anything could and probably will happen with the Blues, and that’s not a compliment.

6 Everton The Toffees should be worse than last season – Iliman Ndiaye has left, Jack Grealish issued a grovelling apology after going awol, their only striker is Thierno Barry – but instead they are much, much better. David Moyes, the genius grandpa of the league at 63, will set a new Everton record for games managed this weekend (593).

7 Bournemouth The Dorset Hydra from Greek mythology. Every time you cut off its head, or sell a defender to PSG or Real Madrid, they come back stronger. Since Alex Scott was injured on 3 October, Bournemouth lost at Chelsea and then won eight on the bounce. Make it make sense.

8 Brentford This underdog narrative is wearing a little thin now. Brentford are just a well-oiled football machine but they have no mates and worse, no enemies. What a sad little life. Enjoy Tin Pot qualification. We hope it makes you very happy.

9 Leeds The Yorkshire club might be 78% Dominic Calvert-Lewin at this point, so only Lucas Radebe knows where they will be when the striker gets knacked, but Daniel Farke is doing a fine job with Leeds in the top half. For now.

10 Aston Villa It would be hard for any club to lose Morgan Rogers, Ezri Konsa, Ollie Watkins, Youri Tieleman and Emi Martínez in one transfer window and improve. Not much more to say. Why do we like Zion Suzuki so much? Genuinely not sure.

11 Tottenham Whisper it, but are Spurs back (to being a mid-table team again)?

12 Newcastle One win in six and a kit that looks like a barcode means it’s been a poor campaign thus far, which Matthias Jaissle and co will hope to resurrect this weekend against – checks notes – the champions. Ah.

13 Hull City A remarkable achievement that the promoted side are not anywhere near the bottom three considering there is absolutely nobody, including Sergej Jakirovic, who can name 11 current Hull players off the top of their heads.

14 Ipswich Town Shining bright like Gary O’Neil’s teeth. Keep this up and they might even survive.

15 Fulham Quick tip for Álvaro Arbeloa: when you said on Friday that “Fulham is not a jail – everyone that is here is because they want to be here”, it really makes it sound like Fulham is actually a jail, which nobody wants to be in. Roll on Manchester City (A)!

16 Nottingham Forest It’s entirely believable that Forest will follow up their 3-1 win over Manchester City with victory at Bournemouth this weekend. It’s also entirely believable that Oliver Glasner will be sacked by Christmas and Forest will be playing in the Championship next season. It’s all part of the Evangelos Marinakis Experience, baby.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

17 Coventry City Genuinely quite nice to see that sky blue kit back in the top flight. All Cov fans would snap your hand off for 17th come the end of the campaign.

18 Manchester United Mauricio Pochettino has the chance to do something very, very funny over the next few months.

19 Sunderland Turns out qualifying for Europe isn’t all it’s made up to be.

20 Crystal Palace The Eagles are surely too good to go down? Their trip to Coventry this weekend absolutely stinks of a six-pointer. Hard not to believe that Thomas Frank’s lovely locks will sort out the mess in south London. Imagine if Adam Wharton had Thomas Frank’s hair? OK, that’s enough Football Daily for today.

“My aim was to picture Mauricio Pochettino in his ‌United training kit for the supporters to see for the first time. I fully accept it was unethical on my part. I would like to apologise to Manchester United, Mauricio Pochettino and the players for my actions and sincerely ‌hope those actions do not affect the outcome of any match” – a drone operator who filmed United’s first ⁠training ⁠session under their new head coach at Carrington has apologised for what he called a “total error of judgment”. Welcome to Manchester, Mauricio.

double quotation markI’m a bit late to the party in terms of commenting on the ‘perfume in Poundland’ litigation (Football Daily letters passim). However, I can’t help thinking that David Beckham could have avoided all of this palaver if he’d arranged for Dior to manage his line of fragrances – it seems to have worked out swimmingly for Robbie Sauvage” – Paul Taverner.

double quotation markOh my days, the Memory Lane picture (yesterday’s full email edition) of Eric Gates and Kevin O’Callaghan playing Breakout was surely crying out for an Escape To Victory reference, with the Ipswich and Republic of Ireland winger O’Callaghan famously playing the goalkeeper that had his arm broken to allow Sly Stallone’s character to join the team in their erm … breakout! Nearly time for the traditional Christmas airing of this classic as well” – Dave Griffiths.

If you have any, please send letters to the.boss@theguardian.com. Today’s prizeless letter o’ the day goes to … Dave Griffiths. Terms and conditions for our competitions, when we run them, are here.

Mauricio Pochettino warns Man United cannot be stuck in the past as he sets out his vision for the club - and confirms Bruno Fernandes WILL remain captain amid contract stand-off

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 04:01 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 12:04 EST, 20 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino has warned that Manchester United cannot be stuck in the past as he begins the job of dragging the biggest club in English football out of the Premier League relegation zone.

Pochettino faces a huge first game in charge against Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday, and he is determined to make a fresh start at United uninhibited by the history of the club.

‘We arrived with the idea to create something from scratch, from our own ideas and philosophy, in the way that we love to work. I think our job is to adapt and to go ahead a little bit,’ he said at Carrington.

‘The culture of the club and the identity of the club is very clear. It's an historic club with years being one of the best clubs in the world. It's difficult to find another club the size of Manchester United. It needs to be in a different place than we are now, no?

‘But I don't think too much in the past. If we want to create something different and positive, and the people feel proud about that, it's about building our own way here. Thinking on what happened yesterday is not going to help. You cannot be stuck in the past.’

Pochettino’s appointment at United has been a whirlwind after the Argentine oversaw his final two games in charge of the US national team before flying through the night to start work on Wednesday.

Mauricio Pochettino spoke to the media for the first time as Man United boss on Friday

‘I think it's a race to arrive in condition to be competitive against Liverpool, and now our focus is 100 per cent on Liverpool,’ he added.

‘My first target is to sleep, but I cannot sleep – only three or four hours per day – but I think the target is to know the people, the staff, all the people here in the club, and of course the players and try from the beginning to have a positive impact.’

Pochettino admits he has missed being involved in day-to-day coaching at club level, saying: ‘Of course, we missed it a lot. We are animals of the grass. We like to feel the grass, we need to smell the grass and to help the players to improve every day.

‘It's true that it's a different job when you are in the national team, but I think we are much better coaches now.’

The 54-year-old told United that he wants to be involved in the all the key decision-making and transfers despite only being named as the club’s head coach.

Pochettino demanded more control during his five years in charge of Tottenham, and the difference between manager and head coach has also been a bone of contention at United in recent years.

Manchester United travel to rivals Liverpool on Sunday searching for their first league win since August

Ruben Amorim was sacked after taking on the United hierarchy over his job title, while Erik ten Hag refused to give up his power to veto transfers.

Pochettino said: ‘I know that being a head coach, I am the guy that is going to decide the squad for Sunday and I'm going to pick the starting 11.

‘I'm going to make the changes and of course that, in the future, I would like to be involved in all the main decisions that will happen in the squad. That is (why) in the end, Manchester United is a football club, no?

‘During all my (career), when I started at Espanyol, head coach, coach, manager. In the end, it's not changing too much because I think the way that we work is indifferent to the title description.

‘If you are manager it’s not necessarily that you have all the decisions. Maybe you are a coach and have more power in the decision.’

Pochettino revealed that he met most of the players individually at Carrington on Thursday, including captain Bruno Fernandes who cut a frustrated figure towards the end of Michael Carrick’s time in charge.

Pochettino confirmed that out-of-form Bruno Fernandes will remain as United captain

‘Bruno is our captain and I think he's a very important player, but all the players need to feel that they are important from the top to the bottom,’ said Pochettino.

‘Yesterday we spent three hours chatting in an individual way in my office with all the players.’

Concerns have been raised over the players’ fitness and attitude under Carrick this season, with the statistics suggesting that United have not been running enough.

‘That's a good question, no?,’ said Pochettino. ‘It’s a debate that I don't like to go into because I think we cannot talk about things without context, and data is about context. I think it's very dangerous to talk. We are here not to denounce things, we are here to build something.’

Giants rumors: Insider leaves door open for Rafael Devers offseason trade

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Giants rumors: Insider leaves door open for Rafael Devers offseason trade appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The San Francisco Giants need to make some sort of change. They have a number of star players on their roster, but success has been difficult to find for this ball club. Trading a veteran or two this offseason may be a path they decide to take — if they can move any of their big contracts. Rafael Devers' strong 2026 season may cause teams to have interest in him despite the fact that he is still owed $211 million on his current contract. MLB insider Mark Feinsand echoed that sentiment in a recent article for MLB.com.

After admitting that trading him won't be easy due to the contract, Feinsand added that his impressive 2026 performance has set up a situation in which "a number of teams would love to add his impact bat to their lineup."

The financial element of the trade would need to be discussed, as the Giants may need to take on some of the money owed to Devers. Nevertheless, a trade is seemingly not out of the question — despite the fact that some have suggested Devers won't be moved.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Devers, 30, hit .258 to go along with an .865 OPS and 37 home runs this past season. He's capable of playing first or third base and is also a candidate to be a team's designated hitter. There are plenty of ball clubs searching for corner infield help who would benefit from acquiring a star such as Devers.

One has to wonder if teams such as the New York Yankees or Philadelphia Phillies would inquire about a potential Devers trade. Devers is probably now at his best defensively while playing first base or DH, but the Yankees are in need of a third baseman. There could be a discussion there. Meanwhile, the Phillies may want to bring in a first baseman if Bryce Harper is prepared to remain in the outfield.

It is uncertain if either of those ball clubs have interest. Only time will tell if the Giants are able to make a Devers-led deal this offseason, but the door is seemingly open for a trade.

Hard-luck Rams catch break in Puka Nacua suspension

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It figures that in a season this sideways, the best break the Rams have caught in weeks came from the people who just suspended their best player.

Somewhere in the NFL offices, somebody signed off on a three-game suspension for Puka Nacua. Somewhere in Los Angeles, somebody had to look at the calendar and think, well, if you insist.

Los Angeles Rams wide receiver Puka Nacua reacts after winning an NFL football game against the Washington Commanders in Landover, Md., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Stephanie Scarbrough

Nacua can serve all three games while he's on injured reserve. The shoulder pads were going to stay in his locker anyway. When he's healthy enough to put them back on, his suspension will already be behind him.

How often this season have the Rams been able to say something worked out about as well as they could have hoped?

Certainly nobody was saying it after the Denver game. The Rams had a game they should have won, the kind you circle when you're trying to figure out how a team with this much talent ended up having to fight this hard just to keep its head above water.

Just look at who has been hit by injuries. Myles Garrett. Nacua. Davante Adams. Cornerbacks Jaylen Watson and Trent McDuffie. That's a whole lot of the blueprint spending time on the injury report. The Rams built this team to pressure quarterbacks, cover receivers and make defenses account for two dangerous targets.

Injuries have reached into every one of those plans. Sean McVay has spent too much of this season figuring out what's left to work with each week.

They've also had more experience than anybody around here wanted finding out what the offense looks like with one of its best players standing next to McVay. There's a reason a coach spends all week drawing up ways to get the ball to somebody. The guy behind him might know exactly where to line up. There's still the matter of what happens once the ball gets there.

With Nacua, sometimes the best part of the play starts right about where it should end. A defender gets his arms around him a yard short of the sticks, and somehow it's first down. Try finding that on the call sheet.

That still leaves the Packers, the Chiefs and the 49ers on the schedule without Nacua. Go ahead and read those names back to McVay and see how lucky he feels.

Head Coach Sean McVay of the Los Angeles Rams looks on during the fourth quarter against the Washington Commanders in Landover, Maryland, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Except the Rams were going to play those games without him anyway. And that's where the calendar did them a favor. They've already been figuring out how to win without Nacua. Now they just have to do it a little longer.

Maybe the catches come from three different guys against Green Bay. Maybe the running backs have to carry them against Kansas City. By the time San Francisco rolls around, nobody will care if the same formula worked twice.

Now imagine getting word that Nacua is healthy again and having to explain to the rest of the locker room that it'll be another three Sundays before anybody gets to see him play. That was the danger. The timing of the suspension took it off the table.

At some point on that flight, everybody involved had to let go of whatever they thought the mission was going to look like. They had duct tape. They had some awfully smart people on the ground. They had a bunch of stuff on board originally intended for other purposes. What could they piece together that would get three astronauts back to Earth?

Get there however you can. Win an ugly one. Ask a backup to give you one good afternoon. Give the defense a lead late and let somebody on that side close out the kind of game that got away against Denver. Then come back Monday and see who can practice.

For all that's gone sideways, the Rams are still alive. And the team an opponent draws in January could look a whole lot more like the one we expected in September than the one McVay is trying to hold together in November.

Puka Nacua #12 of the Los Angeles Rams celebrates a fourth-quarter touchdown with Head Coach Sean McVay in Landover, Maryland, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Nobody asks how much duct tape it took once you land. And if the Rams get there with Nacua healthy, good luck to the defensive coordinator who spent the last month watching film of an offense that had to make do without him.

All the Rams need is a way in. From there, they have a chance to run the table. Funny to think, after everything that's happened to them, who just helped keep the door open.

Houston Rockets Star Tari Eason Sues Ex Over Stolen Chrome Hearts, Rolex And Louis Vuitton

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Tari Eason is bringing his breakup battle back to court ... this time claiming his ex-girlfriend poured bleach on his fancy clothes and made off with a small fortune in luxury goods … TMZ has learned.

According to court documents, obtained by TMZ ... the Houston Rockets forward is pursuing claims against his former girlfriend, Marilyn Maritza Melo Rodriguez, alleging she entered his rental property without permission in June, destroyed a bunch of his clothes by pouring bleach on them, and swiped several expensive belongings.

Eason claims Marilyn made off with a $15,000 Louis Vuitton duffel bag, a Rolex watch, Chrome Hearts glasses, and other valuables ... and has refused to return them despite repeated demands from him and his attorney.

The NBA baller claims he's suffered at least $92,000 in losses, including the value of the damaged clothing ... and is seeking up to triple that amount in damages under California law, plus additional amounts and attorneys' fees.

Eason is in his fifth year in the NBA ... averaging 10.4 points and 6.3 rebounds per game in his career.

As we previously reported ... Eason sought a domestic violence restraining order against Marilyn in July, alleging she punched him in the face and destroyed thousands of dollars in designer clothes with bleach.

A source close to Marilyn previously disputed Eason's allegations ... telling TMZ his account was false and misleading.

We're told the temporary restraining order remains in effect, with a hearing scheduled for July 29, 2027.

Now, Eason wants his expensive belongings back ... or the money to replace them.

Houston Texans vs. Indianapolis Colts ‘TNF’ Week 11 live game chat

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Look what we have here, Rumblers: It's a primetime matchup between a pair of AFC teams. Though we've had a couple this season and more frequently of late, AFC vs. AFC is about as rare as a Barbary lion these days. The trouble here is that this is a terrible matchup, featuring teams that are decidedly anything other than apex hunters.

That the NFL can still sell this product to anyone is an incredible feat. Yet football fans continue lining up to see awful teams take on each other. It's not that this one is without some interest, which is due to a few star players among both Houston and Indianapolis.

The issue is that as Buffalo Bills fans, you're settling in to watch the 3-6 Texans take on the 3-7 Colts. Those two teams are a combined 6-13 — which, if you've done the math, is less total wins than the 8-1 Bills have on the season.

Neither of these teams was supposed to flop this season, though the writing was on the wall for Indianapolis. But Houston? It's been an epic collapse for the Texans in 2026. A once-mighty defense has proven feckless far too often this season, which has some saying that head coach DeMeco Ryans could be warming the very seat he sits on in Houston. Fair or not, the NFL is a production-based industry.

If you're like me, the game will be on simply because it helps fill the room a bit this evening. You may also share my passion for the Thursday Night Football crew, and their pre-/postgame coverage. If nothing else, there's also the chances to see the Texans in their Nike Rivals uniforms — and yes, they sure did again steal the Oilers' vibe.

Are you looking for a reason to root on one team over the other here? What you favor depends on who to cheer on to victory. A Houston win helps Buffalo's strength of schedule/strength of victory, which matters most for NFL teams with postseason hopes on life support. Instead, you may prefer seeing the Colts win simply so it "harms" the slotting of 2027 first-round pick they sent to the New York Jets.

With that, here's your open thread for Week 11 of Thursday Night Football. Maybe this one also turns into a decent game, like a few suspect snooze-fests in primetime before it. As always, be kind to one another — and in all things… Go Bills!

Variety Awards Circuit section is the home for all awards news and related content throughout the year, featuring the following: the official predictions for the upcoming Oscars, Emmys, Grammys and Tony Awards ceremonies, curated by Variety chief awards editor Clayton Davis. The prediction pages reflect the current standings in the race and do not reflect personal preferences for any individual contender. As other formal (and informal) polls suggest, competitions are fluid and subject to change based on buzz and events. Predictions are updated every Thursday.

2027 Academy Awards Predictions Commentary All Categories (Updated: Nov. 19, 2026): Members of the Oscars are going into the Thanksgiving holiday with some homework to do, but they seem to have a clear road map for where to start.

One of the final major variables of the fall, Netflix’s “The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth,” premiered at the TCL Chinese Theatre on Nov. 16. Early reactions have been largely enthusiastic. That leaves the David Fincher-directed, Quentin Tarantino-penned film in roughly the same position it occupied heading into the premiere, but maybe it can pick up some heat.

The awards calendar picks up quickly this week.

On Monday, the Critics Choice artisan shortlists will be released, offering the first meaningful read on the below-the-line races. That’s where “The Odyssey” and “Dune: Part Three” go head-to-head in production design, cinematography, sound and visual effects, while a scrappier contender could make its case before the guilds weigh in.

Then comes the season’s first major awards night: the Gotham Awards on Nov. 30. “The Invite” is among five films tied for the most nominations, with four apiece and shares the best feature lineup with “The Debut.” Both A24 Films appear on the Variety best picture list.

And then there’s the buzz still swirling around Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim’s “You Can See Everything.” For the first time this year, Amanda Seyfried takes the top spot in supporting actress and could be headed to a historic triumph if the buzz continues to grow. Also sitting in the No. 1 spot for the first time this year is Matt Damon’s work as Odysseus in Nolan’s “The Odyssey.”

Oh yeah, and Robert Pattinson is James Bond.

The announcement lands in the middle of the most acclaimed stretch of Pattinson’s career, with four films in contention for attention. But why does a casting announcement matter to an Oscar race? Because Oscars can be won on a single narrative.

Brendan Fraser (“The Whale”) had the comeback, same as Ke Huy Quan (“Everything Everywhere All at Once”). Quan’s co-star Jamie Lee Curtis had the body of work and a town that wanted to reward her. Voters like to feel they’re part of a “moment.”

It’s still early, and there’s still aways to go.

The 17th Governors Awards, honoring Close, Scott, Norman, Vachon and Koffler, take place Sunday, Nov. 15. Preliminary voting for the Oscar shortlists in 12 categories runs Dec. 7-11, with the shortlists announced Tuesday, Dec. 15. Following the close of the eligibility period on Dec. 31, nomination voting runs Jan. 11-15, 2027. The Academy will announce the official Oscar nominations Thursday, Jan. 21, 2027. The 99th Oscars take place Sunday, March 14, 2027, with Conan O’Brien hosting for the third consecutive year. The ceremony airs live at 7 p.m. ET on ABC and in more than 200 territories worldwide from the Dolby Theatre at Ovation Hollywood.

Spain: Pau Cubarsi would like to steal a player from France

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It’s the international break, and all the stars on the planet have joined their selection. For many media, it is an opportunity to interview certain players who are sometimes inaccessible with their club. In Spain, the Federation thus opens the doors to the media enormously. As part of a little game with DAZNPau Cubarsi also left an interesting sequence.

When asked which player he would naturalize for Spain if he had the choice, the Barça and La Roja defender replied Kylian Mbappé. It must be said that the Golden Boy 2026 knows him well, since he has faced him in every Clasico since the summer of 2024…

PCT Weeks 21 & 22: Aftermath

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It was nice to be in a new town. Leavenworth is modeled on a Bavarian village, so I woke up knowing there’d be plenty to check out today. We walked into town to grab breakfast at a cafe that served expensive but delicious waffles, before venturing out to see the sights. It was a Saturday, and with Oktoberfest starting today and a marathon on tomorrow, the town seemed to buzz with a low thrum of energy.

We meandered along the river, and then the streets of the main part of town, which grew increasingly busy as the day went on. We had bratwursts for lunch, and were dismayed when we jumped online and found out that Oktoberfest tickets had sold out. A little dejected, we headed back to the KOA for an afternoon rest. Well, I rested. Sass planned a little DIY Oktoberfest featuring stops at the breweries in town. In the early evening we headed into town to get started. We were still dressed in our trail clothes, so we stuck out like sore thumbs – as soon as we arrived in town we were picked out by locals as PCT hikers. They seemed pretty excited to see us, maybe because most hikers hadn’t made it to Leavenworth this year thanks to the wildfire trail closures.

At the second brewery we went to, we instantly spotted a table of dirtbags. As we waited in line to order, we discussed in hushed tones whether they were thru-hikers or not. Drinks in hand, we walked past their table and discovered they were looking at us with the same curious eyes. Then I recognised a hiker I’d last seen in Big Bear, and all of a sudden we were sitting with them, exchanging news. This has been one of the best parts of trail life: being instant friends with other hikers. They were just finishing their drinks before heading into Oktoberfest. My FOMO was real.

Once they’d gone, and Sass and I had finished our drinks, we decided to at least go and ask if there was a walk-up ticket. And we were in luck! Soon we had cleared security and were on the hunt for our next drink. We paused by one stage where a stein-holding competition was in full swing. The winner really went for it – not satisfied with outlasting his competition, he held that thing up for minutes longer, clearly testing himself. Then we checked out a tent where live music was playing, where we found our friends from earlier and were quickly invited to join them. It was a fun evening of exchanging more trail news, people watching, and singing along to Bavarianised songs. It felt very appropriate that one of them was ‘I’m Gonna Be (500 Miles)’ by the Proclaimers – basically the unofficial anthem of thru-hikers everywhere.

Sass and I had planned to thru-hike the Enchantments Traverse today, but when we woke up we were feeling the consequences of Oktoberfest. So we decided to postpone until tomorrow, instead. We were in no rush; we were planning to meet Catch in Bend in another week or so, and had plenty of time to kill. Plus, the PCT rate at the KOA was easily the cheapest accommodation we were going to find anywhere.

We spent the morning crawling through coffee shops, then headed to the library to research hire cars to get us down to Bend in a couple of days. Then lunch, and back to the KOA for a few hours of rest before grabbing dinner and turning in early, ready for tomorrow’s hike.

📍 Enchantments Traverse | 🥾 35.65 km | ⛰️ +2,392m / -1,733m

We were awake bright and early, heading into town to pick up a coffee and then trying to hitch up to either of the trailheads for the Enchantments Traverse. Bizarrely, we got a lift with someone we knew! It was a couple we’d run into a couple of times on trail, and who’d been at the terminus with us. They’d been in town last night with friends, but had flights to catch later today so they could only drive us to the trailhead closest to town. Fine by us!

The trail started easily as we climbed well-maintained switchbacks. We made excellent time, and were confident we’d be done with the trail sooner than we’d planned, with plenty of daylight left to get a hitch back to town.

How wrong we were. After passing our first few lakes the trail began to enter tougher territory. Soon we were clambering over boulders, and in a couple of spots we lost the trail for a few minutes, but managed to find our way back. It got steeper and steeper. At the top of every climb I thought: this must be the top of the pass. Wrong. Every time.

At least, once the toughest bit of climbing and navigating was out of the way, the scenery was sublime. The larches were in full, golden splendour, and the clear alpine lakes were glimmering in the sunlight. There wasn’t a cloud in the sky.

Sass cracked his head on a tree and ended up with a pretty decent open wound. Hikers streamed past in the other direction, telling us that Aasgard Pass had been pretty hectic. We brushed them off; we’d just done the PCT, how hard could it be?

Bloody hard, it turns out. There was clearly a reason most people hiked this trail in the opposite direction to us. Aasgard was a steep slope covered in both boulders and loose scree. It was a nightmare to downclimb. It took us at least two hours to get down to Colchuck Lake as we moved slowly, keeping our bodyweight low and trying not to twist an ankle or go sliding with the scree. About a quarter of the way down Sass realised that he’d lost his phone. How frustrating, especially since he had no idea where, and we hadn’t exactly been following a trail since it didn’t really exist amongst the rocks. The chances of finding it were slim. Daylight was fading, too; we didn’t have our overnight gear with us, so had no choice but to keep moving. I suggested we could come back tomorrow to look for it, but he said there was no way he was climbing back up this heinous pass. He just hoped his terminus photos had uploaded to the cloud.

We finally finished the pass and I was ready to rip out the final miles past the lake and down to the trailhead. Only the terrain remained rocky and difficult, and progress was much slower than I’d have liked. We ended up hiking for an hour in the dark, hoping against hope that this wouldn’t stop us from getting a hitch at the trailhead. We were glad to see that the carpark was still pretty much full when we arrived. Surely someone would come by soon. We were in luck: a car approached and Sass waved it down, giving the driver his sob story about his lost phone and head injury, and she immediately offered us a lift to town, which we gratefully accepted.

Today we were headed to Wenatchee, a larger town that had a Verizon store where Sass could get a replacement phone, and we could pick up a hire car. We’d originally planned to head to Mount Rainier and hike the open sections of the Wonderland Trail, but yesterday’s hike had beaten us to a pulp. So now we’d switched plans and were just going to grab the car for a day so we could get to Bend.

Sass had no trouble getting a new phone – and his terminus photos had made it to the cloud! – but the hire car was a different story. Neither of us had credit cards, and none of the hire places in town would rent to us without one. We spent several hours trying to figure out a new plan. Eventually, we found a place in Seattle that would let us hire with a debit card. So all we had to do was get to Seattle. We decided the easiest way would be to get the bus back to Leavenworth tomorrow, and hitch from there, since the locals are pretty used to PCT hikers.

We were up in the dark to catch an early bus to Leavenworth. There, we grabbed a coffee and then headed up to the roundabout on the western side of town to try and hitch. We got a ride up to Stevens Pass pretty quickly, but then we were stuck there with our thumbs out for a long while. Eventually I headed up to the cafe to grab some cardboard and make a hitching sign; but while I was there Sass managed to score us a lift and we were on our way to Seattle.

Our hitch dropped us at a Costco on the outskirts, and from there we took an uber to the car hire place. As we drove along the busy highways I could feel myself growing a little sad. I didn’t like being back in this kind of civilisation. It was too much.

We picked up the car and, too scared to attempt driving on the wrong side of the road, I forced Sass to drive us the 6 hours to Bend. And as I doomscrolled in the passenger seat, I came across a post in the Enchantments hiking Facebook group: someone had found Sass’s phone! What were the chances? I put them in touch, then sat back and enjoyed the views of the mountains we’d hiked by only a couple of weeks ago.

The days we spent in Bend were much the same. Our mornings were spent in one coffee shop or another, catching up on journal entries, going through photos, and doomscrolling. We’d head back to the motel for a supermarket lunch. We’d discovered the Bend Ale Trail app, and in the afternoons we made out our mission to visit as many as we reasonably could. When we grew tired, we headed back to the motel and turned on the tv, catching up on all the releases we’d missed while on trail.

Catch arrived in the evening of Day 150. He’d shaved and changed his clothes, and I hardly recognised him. We spent a good hour catching up on what we’d each been up to for the last week or so, then plotted our moves for this final section up to Government Camp and headed to bed early, eager to be getting back on trail.

📍 Mile 2,025.6 | 🥾 37.89 km | ⛰️ +1,293m / -1,037m

I was not excited about going back to trail today. It was mostly because of the weather. The whole time Sass and I had been waiting around in Bend, the weather had been perfect – up to 27 degrees Celcius. But now, we were walking into a cold snap. Still, my desire to finish these miles won, and soon we were using a combination of Uber and hitching to get to the trailhead.

As we drove, the weather only deteriorated. As we passed through Sisters we were subjected to thick sleet. I decided that if conditions were like this at the trailhead, I’d hitch back to town and never mind the miles. But in the end the weather at the trailhead was ok, if a little windy.

We’d already done the first ten miles previously, but with a light layer of snow on the ground the landscape looked entirely different. I’d been hoping to catch a better view of Three Fingered Jack, which had been largely obscured by smoke last time. But this time it was completely socked in by thick white clouds. We began to descend and were brutalised by the wind and lightly falling snow until we reentered treeline. We chatted from time to time, but it was so chilly that conversations didn’t last long. My lips wouldn’t move properly because of the cold. Our lunch break was only a quick pause to dig out our food, which we ate on the move to keep warm.

I loved walking through the snow, it made everything so delicately beautiful. But I was too cold to bother trying to grab my phone out for photos. As the afternoon started to fade into evening I spotted the snowline on the trees, and began hoping that the campsite we were aiming for would be below it. And I was in luck. Although the spot wasn’t as tree protected as I’d have liked, it had to be warmer than camping in the snow.

📍 Mile 2,058.1 | 🥾 53.08 km | ⛰️ +1,446m / -1,894m

Last night’s temperature hadn’t actually been that bad. The night between days 135 and 136 as we headed to the Canadian border had definitely been worse. It seemed like we were going to have much nicer weather today, too. I could already see glimpses of blue sky as we walked beneath the trees.

Mid-morning, the views started to open up and we caught great sights of Mount Jefferson, clouds dancing around its summit and eventually disappearing in the warm morning sun. Suddenly, I was very glad we’d decided to come back and do this section after all.

The climb up to the border of the Mount Hood Wilderness felt tough, though. I’d expected this section to be flat, as I remembered much of Oregon had been. Once we made it to the top, we scouted out some sun-warmed boulders where we paused briefly for lunch. Unfortunately, the sun wasn’t strong enough to keep us warm for long when we weren’t moving, so it wasn’t long before we were on trail again.

We split up for the afternoon, which I enjoyed. I quite like having the trail to myself. And, at the back of the pack, it was really nice to know it was extremely unlikely that any other hikers would come along behind me. I was free of having to watch out for potential tailgaters.

There was a long burn zone, which was surprisingly rocky and boulder-y underfoot. Just as I was thinking it was a miracle I hadn’t tripped yet – I hadn’t even done one of the usual little toe stubs on a tiny rock – all of a sudden, I was falling. I struggled to regain my balance, but a bush was in the way of my feet and soon I had toppled completely. I just managed to save my face from smashing into a rock, but in the process I fell strangely on my wrist, pinning it underneath me. Ouch was an understatement. It was the same pain I’d felt when I rolled my ankle in NorCal, so I thought I’d probably sprained it. Suddenly, the solitude of the trail at this time of year didn’t seem so nice. I was thankful I had my Garmin inReach. Not that I needed it right now, but it definitely gave me some peace of mind.

I struggled to my feet and continued on, trying my best to keep my wrist above my heart. It slowly began to swell to the size of a grape. Catch and Sass were ahead of me, but I hoped that at some stage they’d have stopped to let me catch up. I’d have liked their help getting my wrist compression wrapped. Worst case, we’d already agreed on a campsite, so I’d see them there.

The rest of the day’s miles were pretty tough. My wrist was searing with pain, and walking with it against my chest wasn’t natural or comfortable. Just after I passed the road into Olallie Lake Resort – which had closed for the season – a car drove past. I seriously considered backtracking to the road and trying to hitch literally anywhere, but in the end stubbornness to finish this section won out and I kept hiking.

I arrived at the campsite in the dark. Catch and Sass had been wondering where I was, so I filled them in, got my wrist strapped, and took a dose of ibuprofen. Then we sat together for a quick dinner before retreating to bed.

📍 Mile 2,089.7 | 🥾 51.59 km | ⛰️ +1,172m / -1,359m

My wrist was still painful in the morning, which I’d anticipated. At least today was our final day on trail and I could look forward to sleeping indoors tonight. Breaking camp took me longer than usual, and I had to ask the guys to help stuff my tent into my pack so I could close it.

Today’s trail was nothing particularly special. We stayed beneath the canopy for pretty much the entire day, so the audiobooks came back out as I plodded along. It was extraordinarily flat, though: I could see now why this section was popular for the 24 hour challenge. I tried not to take too many breaks, eager to be done with trail ASAP. And I ended up walking the last couple of miles at dusk, catching a couple of gorgeous glimpses of a rosy Mount Hood through the trees as I approached the trailhead where Catch had left his car.

It was such a nice change to finish the day with a car waiting at the trailhead. We threw our packs in the back and drove to Government Camp, where we had enough service to search for an Airbnb. In the car, I realised I was properly sick. Sass had gotten a little sick on our last day in Bend, and I’d felt the usual warning sign scratchiness of a sore throat on trail. But it was only once I sat in that car, knowing we were done, that I felt the full force of it. I let the guys take the reins on booking the Airbnb, and soon we were inside, cosy and warm. We hadn’t bothered resupplying – the general store was already closed for the day when we arrived, and we didn’t want to drive further afield – so dinner was trail food. Sass handed me a NyQuil, saying as he did so that they never made him drowsy. But within 20 minutes of starting a movie, I was struggling to keep my eyes open, and had to retreat to bed early.

All three of us were sick as we rotted in front of the tv at our Airbnb for three nights. Although trail was really over now, I don’t think any of us had the energy to actually process it. It was kind of nice to have a good reason not to have to move from the couch, too. We spent our time watching a lot of endurance sports documentaries, venturing into Mount Hood Village only to resupply, and later into Government Camp for the sole purpose of grabbing another of the massive donuts from the Huckleberry Inn. Although on the way we also popped our head into the visitor centre and museum, which ended up having some pretty cool exhibits.

On our last morning together Catch kindly drove us to Portland, where we wandered around a used bookstore to pick up copies of our next book club reads, and then enjoyed a final coffee together. I hadn’t really planned what I was going to do next, and after thinking about it on the drive, I decided to book a flight back to Australia for the next day. I knew I wouldn’t enjoy spending time – or money – in cities on the West Coast after being in the backcountry for so long. So in the afternoon we said goodbye to Catch who was heading off to meet another friend, then Sass and I grabbed a quick dinner and stuffed our checked-luggage-only items into cardboard boxes, ready for our respective flights.

The next morning Sass left early to catch his flight. Mine didn’t leave until 7.30pm, so I hung around the motel until checkout, then ventured into town. I didn’t enjoy it. The hustle and bustle was too much for me, especially on a busy weekend, so I soon made my way back to the motel, collected my pack from behind the counter, and headed to the airport early.

The flight was about as good as any long-haul flight can be. I read a little, watched plenty of movies and tv shows, and had a few decent naps. Then there was a 3.5 hour train, followed by a 2 hour drive home. It was only then that I realised how tired I was, and practically collapsed into bed.

A week passed, and I finally gave into my parents’ nagging and went to the GP about my wrist. She sent me for an x-ray, and I discovered I’d fractured it. Unbelievable. I’ve never broken a bone in my life – unless you count my little toe, which I don’t, really. Earlier on trail we’d all discussed what our worst ever injuries had been, and I hadn’t had any good stories to tell. Now, I do.

Two Directors, One Oscar: Can Lord and Miller, Los Javis, Fielder and Oppenheim or the Esiri Brothers Break Through?

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Awards Circuit Column: The Academy’s Directors Branch has given best director to a duo only three times in 98 years. This season, four teams are vying for attention

Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim, Javier Calvo and Javier Ambrossi, Phil Lord and Chris Miller and Nigeria’s Esiri brothers are chasing a prize the Academy’s Director Branch has handed to a duo only three times in its history.

Looking at the two-name directing teams in this year’s Oscar race, artistic collaboration seems to be having a moment. Not everyone sees it that way. Related Stories 'Dhurandhar,' 'Kantara: A Legend – Chapter 1' Among Indian Panorama Lineup at IFFI Goa Jazz Documentary 'Tokyo in 33 RPM' Unveiled Ahead of India's WAVES Film Bazaar (EXCLUSIVE)

“If you need two of you to make the film, then I think that’s a problem,” one prominent member of the Directors Branch bluntly tells Variety.

Fielder and Oppenheim turned Elizabeth Holmes’ saga into the slippery A24 documentary “You Can See Everything.” Calvo and Ambrossi, the Spanish duo known as Los Javis, are carrying their country’s international feature hopes with “La Bola Negra.” Lord and Miller reunited behind the camera for Amazon MGM’s sci-fi swing “Project Hail Mary.” And twin brothers Arie and Chuko Esiri helmed Neon’s ambitious “Clarissa,” a reimagining of “Mrs. Dalloway.”

All four teams face a stubborn piece of Academy history: The Directors Branch does not like splitting statuettes. In 98 years, only four directing duos have received best director nominations (one of them twice). This year, several are within earshot of nods.

Robert Wise and Jerome Robbins got there first for 1961’s “West Side Story.” Their partnership was so combustible that Robbins was fired mid-production for running over schedule and budget. He retained his credit and collected the Oscar.

Warren Beatty and Buck Henry followed with “Heaven Can Wait” (1978), but lost to Michael Cimino (“The Deer Hunter”). Joel and Ethan Coen scored for the best picture winner “No Country for Old Men” (2007), and were nominated again for the Western remake “True Grit” (2010), losing out to Tom Hooper (“The King’s Speech”). The most recent winners, Daniel Kwan and Daniel Scheinert, aka the Daniels, took home the prize for “Everything Everywhere All at Once” (2022).

History suggests that longtime professional partnerships are the key to acceptance. Wise and Robbins were a filmmaker and a choreographer brought together by a studio. The Daniels met making music videos. The Coens remain the only sibling duo recognized.

The Academy has repeatedly overlooked pairings, even in the face of precursor support. Jonathan Dayton and Valerie Faris, the married filmmakers behind “Little Miss Sunshine” (2006), won SAG ensemble and PGA honors and earned a DGA feature nom. The Oscars’ directing branch still shut them out.

The Safdie brothers were similarly snubbed for “Uncut Gems” (2019), but when Josh Safdie made his solo debut with “Marty Supreme” last year, the branch gave him a nod. Even the Wachowskis couldn’t crack the category for the revolutionary technical achievement of “The Matrix” (1999).

This season’s teams fit familiar types. Lord and Miller and Fielder and Oppenheim resemble the Daniels’ professional partnership, while the Esiri siblings are following the Coens’ path. Los Javis are the wild card: The couple’s roughly 13-year romantic relationship ended while they were making “La Bola Negra,” their most personal and ambitious collaboration.

“The first question other directors will ask is ‘Why did you need two people to do this?’” one veteran strategist says. “The job is to convince a bunch of directors who often think they are the most creative artists alive that their genius can be shared. That’s tough.”

None of the four teams is a conventional frontrunner. Fielder and Oppenheim are competing in a documentary lane the Directors Branch has always kept separate. Los Javis are more likely to make noise in international feature and screenplay because theirs is a non-English-language film. Lord and Miller are the closest thing to a studio-scale contender, but genre films need to overperform in below-the-line races to enter the conversation.

Still, the sheer number of teams is the story. Four duos in one year reflects an industry increasingly built on shared creative enterprise, even as many directors cling to the myth of the solo genius.

This week’s updated predictions are below.

(In alphabetical order. Ranked choices are on each individual category page.)

Lakers’ Official History Book Gets $9,600 Limited Edition With Magic Johnson Handprints, Staples Center Hardwood and Rare Artwork

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Variety may receive an affiliate commission.

The Los Angeles Lakers’ $100 coffee-table book is getting a five-figure upgrade.

Luxury art-book publisher Taschen and the Lakers have unveiled two limited-edition versions of “Lakers: An Official History of the Purple & Gold,” featuring original handprints from Showtime-era legends, authenticated hardwood from the former Staples Center court and exclusive artwork signed by Magic Johnson and Kareem Abdul-Jabbar. Related Stories 'Ramayana,' 'Fatherland,' 'Dhurandhar' Lead International Film Festival of India Lineup Featuring Almodovar, Kore-eda, Kurosawa Jazz Documentary 'Tokyo in 33 RPM' Unveiled Ahead of India's WAVES Film Bazaar (EXCLUSIVE)

The new releases, announced Thursday, are limited to 1,100 copies worldwide, with 1,000 Collector’s Editions priced at $2,400 each and just 100 Art Editions retailing for $9,600.

Both versions include the original 528-page book, housed in a custom leather clamshell box designed by Jeff Hamilton, the fashion designer known for his elaborate leather jackets commemorating the Lakers’ championship victories in 2000, 2001, 2002 and 2020.

Each box also contains an authenticated piece of hardwood from the original Staples Center floor, where the Lakers won five championships and Kobe Bryant played his final NBA game. Collectors will receive an original handprint from one of seven Showtime-era figures — Johnson, Abdul-Jabbar, James Worthy, Michael Cooper, Kurt Rambis, Byron Scott or Pat Riley — with the identity revealed upon delivery.

The $9,600 Art Edition takes things a step further, adding a large-format print of Ernie Barnes’ “Fastbreak,” originally commissioned by the Lakers in 1987. The 41-by-24.5-inch artwork depicts the team in the midst of its Showtime era and features seven signature plates hand-signed by the players and coach portrayed in the painting. Each print is numbered and authenticated by the Estate of Ernie Barnes.

The limited editions arrive nearly two months after Taschen released the standard version of the book in September. Abdul-Jabbar wrote the foreword, Lakers governor Jeanie Buss contributed the introduction and former coach Phil Jackson penned the afterword. Other contributors include Johnson, O’Neal, Pau Gasol, LeBron James, Luka Dončić, Jack Nicholson, Paula Abdul, Flea and Lou Adler.

“The Lakers have had an extraordinary impact on basketball and pop culture, and we wanted this book to capture the full scope of that influence, from the game itself to art, fashion, music, and beyond,” Tobak said in a statement. “The Limited Edition takes that vision a step further, bringing together rare memorabilia, artwork, and personal contributions from the people who helped shape the team’s history.”

Both limited editions are available now through Taschen’s website.
