Premier League: 10 things to look out for this weekend

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bruno Guimarães to feel the heat on Tyneside, Alex Scott’s return further cheers Cherries and Manchester City face favourite whipping boys

After three Premier League defeats on the spin, Manchester City need something or someone to come along and put some pep in their step, and Fulham look like just the thing – after all, in the past 15 years these teams have played 20 times in all competitions, and City have won precisely 20 of those games. Their current run of 17 successive league wins against Fulham is the longest run of victories by one club against a single opponent in English top-flight history. It is a reflection of the changing realities of English football, and the gulf that has opened between the teams that dominate it and those who seek to challenge them, that of the top 10 such runs all but one occurred in whole or in part after 2010 and six of them have been achieved by Sheikh Mansour’s Manchester City, with Chelsea responsible for another two, Manchester United one, and Portsmouth’s postwar, 13-game supremacy over Everton – a wild ride that included two 5-0s, a 5-1, a 6-3 and a 7-0 – the anomaly. Simon Burnton

Manchester City v Fulham, Saturday 12.30pm (all times GMT)

Positivity surrounds Aston Villa despite them failing to win in four matches, the last of which ended in agonising fashion; they had to settle for a 4-4 draw at Manchester United after twice squandering a two-goal advantage. Villa should have managed the final minutes better, but a home date with Sunderland presents an opportunity to return to winning ways. Unai Emery needs to eliminate the defensive naivety that has blighted some performances and the expected absence of Matty Cash, this time owing to concussion sustained on Poland duty, would deny the Villa manager his preferred defensive lineup. This fixture threw up a classic last season, Tammy Abraham scoring a stoppage-time winner to extend the visitors’ miserable record at Villa Park. Sunderland have won on only two of their past 17 league visits, their last victory coming in 2011 courtesy of the former Villa loanee Phil Bardsley. Ben Fisher

The last time Alex Scott played for Bournemouth, Marco Rose was yet to taste victory in the Premier League. When the 23-year-old returns to midfield he will do so with the team in a totally different place. Bournemouth have racked up eight straight wins in all competitions before hosting Nottingham Forest on Saturday and Scott, who has been able to build his fitness during the international break after a thigh problem, may have to wait his turn owing to the form of Tyler Adams and Ryan Christie. If Rose opts for momentum, then Eli Junior Kroupi, fresh from scoring his first France goal, could make his first start of the season after sustaining a freak foot injury in pre-season. Bournemouth have hardly been short of firepower, though, with Evanilson scoring seven goals in his past eight games. BF

Bournemouth v Nottingham Forest, Saturday 3pm

Chelsea left Wearside battered and bruised after being pushed around by Brian Brobbey. The Sunderland forward bullied Wesley Fofana and Levi Colwill, who had to be substituted at half-time, and won the game’s decisive penalty with a solo run that left Xabi Alonso angry with his side’s powderpuff defending. Alonso insisted Chelsea had prepared for Brobbey’s threat; the Chelsea manager will hope they are better at putting the plan into practice when they face another target man on Saturday. Leeds head to Stamford Bridge and will look to Dominic Calvert-Lewin, fresh from helping England get over the line against Spain last weekend, to unnerve Alonso’s defenders. Jacob Steinberg

Thomas Frank’s first assessment of his new charges after taking over at Crystal Palace this week included the nuggets that “we’re conceding too many goals … we need to work on the defensive side of things with principles, attitude, everything regarding that”, and in particular “we need to work on the set pieces, that’s key”. If he can’t inspire an immediate improvement this is where this match could be decided, being as it is an encounter between the league’s worst set-piece defenders – no one comes close to the 67 shots and xG of 7.44 coughed up by Palace at restarts – and the best. Though Coventry have let in one goal from set pieces, and neither Brentford nor Liverpool have shipped any, the hosts’ set-piece xG of 1.65 is a league low, and they were equally outstanding from dead-ball situations in the Championship last season. The home side will be boosted by the return of the 6ft 4in defender Bobby Thomas, who, despite a calf injury that meant he missed the last two games and the final third of the one before that, has still made more clearances than any other Coventry player. SB

It may have been a slightly strange week for Tottenham fans. They have watched as their former managers Mauricio Pochettino and Thomas Frank have been installed at Manchester United and Crystal Palace respectively, while their current head coach, Roberto De Zerbi, picked up the Premier League’s manager of the month award for October. That is reflective of the improvement De Zerbi has overseen in recent weeks, with Spurs happy to move forward with the Italian and avoid the chaos enveloping some of their rivals. The long-held assumption among some connected to Tottenham that Pochettino would one day return to save the club must now be banished; De Zerbi needs full buy-in from everyone to build his project. That’s the only way Spurs can ensure they steer clear of future crises. Dominic Booth

With the temperature in Newcastle forecast to dip to 0C at kick-off, Matthias Jaissle is perhaps hoping that his team’s guests from north London (where a balmy 9C is predicted at 5.30pm) freeze in the Tyneside chill. Otherwise it is hard to envisage his young side overcoming a formidable looking Arsenal on a day when Newcastle’s head coach seeks what would be only his second win in seven games. Home fans will not merely be hurt by the sight of their much-missed former captain Bruno Guimarães wearing Arsenal colours but decidedly nervous about a fixture that precedes a run of tricky engagements involving a trip to Brighton and home dates with Manchester United and Sunderland. Jaissle’s cause could be helped should Mikel Arteta name Guimarães in his starting XI and the crowd respond to the once adored Brazilian’s defection by creating the sort of hostile atmosphere that might just galvanise a Newcastle team expected to contain the former Arsenal midfielder Joe Willock. Meanwhile Jaissle’s defence must be mindful of the threat posed by another Newcastle old boy in Mikel Merino. Louise Taylor

Brighton are out to prove their early-season form has been no fluke and the fixture list for November and December looks promising for their chances of retaining their lofty position. Hull, having started the season with back-to-back league victories, now appear vulnerable and have not won since that August burst. Brighton follow the trip to Humberside with a home game against Universitatea Craiova in the Conference League, then host Newcastle, and face none of the big six until a visit to Tottenham on 29 December. With Brighton having won seven of their 10 league games, tentative talk of a title challenge may not seem so fanciful come Christmas. The challenge tactically for Fabian Hürzeler’s men will be to deal with the tag of favourites for most of their upcoming games, with teams perhaps starting to wise up to Brighton’s high-pressing, vertical style. DB

And so it starts all over again. Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager in 13 years – not forgetting the interim spells of Ryan Giggs, Michael Carrick, Ralf Rangnick and Darren Fletcher – was impressively quick to commit to the cause when taking a private jet from Cincinnati to Manchester immediately after his final game as USA head coach. Mauricio Pochettino needs his new, floundering team to reciprocate at Anfield. United have conceded four times in the first four minutes of a game this season, seven goals in the opening 17 minutes in total, and will have had little time to absorb instructions from their latest coach by the time they face an expectant Liverpool. United’s defensive options might tempt many managers to board the next flight out but, as a decent record against the so-called big six since the start of last season demonstrates, the team are capable of showing up when they feel like it. That is just one of the many issues Pochettino has to address. Andy Hunter

Liverpool v Manchester United, Sunday 4.30pm

Fans of neutral officiating will appreciate the fact that both Brentford and Everton have won six games refereed by Craig Pawson, who will take charge of this match. But where the two clubs’ experiences diverge is that, while Brentford have only danced to the tune of the Pawson whistle 12 times, enjoying a 50% win ratio, Everton have toiled through 33 Pawson-helmed matches for an equivalent figure of 18%. Of Everton’s past 14 Pawson-patrolled matches there has been only one victory, and 10 defeats. Pawson, meanwhile, has taken charge of five Brentford home matches and they have won every one, thanks in part to the two penalties he has awarded them (both converted), compared with the one he gave against them (missed). Jack Grealish is back having sat out Everton’s past four league games because of illness, lack of fitness and a breach of club discipline, but given that, of the three Premier League matches he has missed, Everton won the two most recent – and the other one was at Arsenal – he may not go straight back into the team. Indeed of Everton’s four league wins this season, Grealish has missed three and played only eight minutes in a fourth. SB

Liverpool hold initial talks with Alexis Mac Allister over a new deal

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have held initial talks with Alexis Mac Allister’s representatives over a potential new deal at Anfield, according to a report from Ben Jacobs.

Jacobs suggested on Wednesday that new Liverpool sporting director Julian Ward has spoken with both Mac Allister’s agent and father a possible contract extension.

Watching Andoni Iraola get a new era underway at Anfield following his arrival over the summer, the Argentinian international has been one of the key performances in the red half of Merseyside this season.

Heading into November’s international break with a maiden Premier League strike of the season during Liverpool’s 3-1 victory away at Crystal Palace, Mac Allister has become a key figure in Iraola’s side.

However, with his current deal set to expire in 2028, the former Brighton star has made his frustrations clear in regards to finding a solution for his long-term future.

With the Anfield hierarchy previously in no rush to tie the 27-year-old down to a new deal, they passed on the opportunity to open official discussions during the summer.

Nevertheless, with the former Premier League champions making it clear that they door was never shut on Mac Allister signing a renewal, Jacobs reported on Wednesday that Liverpool have finally held initials talks with the 59-cap international.

🚨 BREAKING: New Liverpool sporting director Julian Ward has had some initial contact with Alexis Mac Allister’s father and agent over a new deal.

Discussions are exploratory talks at this point.

Mac Allister has started the season extremely well and has proven an important player for Andoni Iraola.

Mac Allister’s contract runs to 2028. Talks are centred around whether a renewal is viable for both parties.

#LFC in no rush, and were not inclined to make a formal offer over the summer.

The door was never totally shut to a Mac Allister renewal as Liverpool had never ended dialogue, but now conversations are more framed around specifics.

Who is supporting Gianni Infantino and how many votes will secure re-election as Fifa president?

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Infantino needs to reach the magic number of 106 votes to secure re-election next March

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Gianni Infantino is facing a battle to cling onto his Fifa presidency heading into next year’s election after his botched bid to sell off stakes in the World Cup to private investors resulted in a significant loss in support.

England and Wales have been among the football associations to join European governing body Uefa in leading the charge for Infantino’s removal from power, formally withdrawing their endorsement of the Swiss-Italian for re-election in March 2027.

But as the dust has settled on his privatisation ploy, Infantino’s bid for a third term has strengthened. His allies have surfaced, with the likes of Mexico and Argentina joining Caf, the African football governing body, in reaffirming support for the under-fire president, and now the Asian Football Confederation (AFC) has reversed its position and backed Infantino with AFC president Sheikh Salman bin Ibrahim Al Khalifa underlining that there is “a true understanding between us”.

Infantino does have his challengers, with French Football Federation president Philippe Diallo, former Switzerland captain Ramon Vega and Japan Football Association honorary president Kozo Tashima all joining the race. The deadline to submit presidential bids passed on 18 November.

But Infantino, confident of staying on for a fourth term, laughed in response to a question over whether he had considered resigning as president, declaring he retains the support of the “vast, vast majority” of world football.

Infantino needs 106 votes from the 211 member associations to be re-elected as president. Here is every country and confederation to have expressed their opposition or support for Infantino:

Uefa, the governing body for Europe, has staunchly opposed Infantino’s leadership. They threatened a boycott of Fifa competitions before Infantino’s eventual climbdown but has continued to assert that his position is untenable, even taking legal action against the Swiss-Italian.

The body points to how there have not been “assurances” that attempts like the privatisation of the World Cup “to disfigure the game in this way will never be made again”.

Uefa general secretary Theodore Theodoridis has written to his Fifa counterpart Mattias Grafstrom setting out a series of questions on behalf of European members of the Fifa Council, demanding documents covering a series of Infantino controversies. Fifa has until 2 November to answer these questions.

Concacaf, the Confederation for North America and the Caribbean, came out in opposition of Infantino after being left stunned at the lack of consultation during the Fifa sell-off plan, which they went on to reject.

After the plan was aborted, the confederation said: “This recent unilateral and egregious act of poor governance and leadership follows a pattern of missteps and similar behaviour. A comprehensive reckoning with this presidency is imperative.”

Concacaf president Victor Montagliani was initially seen as a front-runner to challenge Infantino in next March’s election but he now looks set to pursue another term as Concacaf chief.

The Albanian Football Federation aligned itself with Uefa.

“The Albanian Football Association has sent an official letter to Fifa Secretary General Mattias Grafström, informing him that it has withdrawn its support for Mr Gianni Infantino’s candidacy for another four-year term as Fifa President,” the statement confirmed.

Rinse and repeat for Austria’s federation, who said “a united approach by the European associations sends a strong signal of responsibility for the sport and of solidarity.”

Belgium, who felt the brunt of another Infantino controversy at the World Cup over Folarin Balogun’s red card, have withdrawn their support for his re-election. citing Fifa’s failure to demonstrate “any transparency” under his leadership.

As did the Croatian Football Federation (HNS): "The Croatian Football Federation has informed Fifa of its decision to withdraw the support previously expressed for Mr Gianni Infantino's candidature for another term as Fifa president.

"This decision followed a thorough reassessment within the federation in light of recent developments surrounding the Fifa World Cup 2026 and discussions within our governing bodies. We consider our position to be clearly communicated to Fifa and have no further comment to add at this time."

The Cyprus Football Association (CFA) aligned itself with Uefa’s position and said it felt Infantino could no longer continue as president.

"Under the weight of what has taken place and under today's conditions, the CFA finds that the necessary prerequisites are no longer in place for the President of Fifa to continue exercising his duties," the association said.

Denmark did not endorse Infantino’s re-election bid prior to the scheme’s reveal and haven’t changed their stance.

There is also a precedent for their opposition to Infantino, with federation president Jesper Moller not supporting his re-election efforts back in 2022: “There is only one candidate ... but Denmark will not be supporting the current president.”

The English FA is one of many European member associations to send a letter to Fifa withdrawing its support for Infantino’s re-election in 2027.

Infantino’s apology letter changed nothing for the FA, who was “deeply concerned about the lack of process and governance” surrounding his sell-off scheme.

All the Nordic nations ended up joining forces in a combined statement, to say they had “lost confidence” in Infantino’s leadership. This included the Faroe Islands.

Finland kickstarted the process of formal withdrawals, with it being revealed that the Scandinavian member association was the first to send a letter officially withdrawing support for Infantino.

France withdrew their endorsement of Infantino in September and proposed to seek ways to transform how Fifa is run.

"A project with such a significant impact on the future of football could not be envisaged without comprehensive information, in-depth consultation and a fully transparent decision-making process," FFF president Philippe Diallo said in a statement, who is now running against Infantino as Uefa’s last-minute candidate. "Fifa's governance does not live up to the standards that football represents."

The Georgian Football Federation similarly withdrew its support for Infantino’s candidacy.

The German federation (DFB) already opted against endorsing Infantino earlier in the year so has not had to send a letter withdrawing support to take an opposing stance.

“The DFB has not signed a letter of support for the re-election of Gianni Infantino," the governing body stated in July. "Further steps will be discussed in the DFB presidential board."

Former Germany national team director Oliver Bierhoff further reiterated the nation’s loss of trust in Infantino: "We have to take care of football. It is already commercial but it is a property for all of football. The world views may be different but in the last weeks and months a lot happened so that we have no trust any more that it will be going in the right direction."

Another smaller footballing nation to withdraw its endorsement, Gibraltar aligned itself with Uefa.

“Accordingly, the Gibraltar FA has decided that it can no longer maintain its endorsement of Mr Infantino’s candidature for the 2027 Fifa presidential election. Fifa has been formally informed of that decision,” a statement read.

The Greek Football Association has backed Uefa’s push for a change in Fifa’s leadership, similarly withdrawing support to underscore a united European front in opposition to Infantino.

"The Greek FA (EPO) has taken a clear position against a candidacy of Gianni Infantino," EPO presdient Makis Gagatsis told reporters. "We saw things happen at the World Cup that are not acceptable to football public opinion. Then... came a programme to sell World Cup rights and we are clearly against it, so we withdrew our support of the Fifa president."

Part of the Nordic collective against the incumbent president, Iceland have also lost confidence in Infantino.

The Israel Football Association was another to formally send a letter to Fifa withdrawing its endorsement of Infantino’s re-election.

In his letter, FA president Moshe Zuares says there was an “unprecedented crisis of confidence” between the visions of Uefa and Fifa when it comes to global governance, adding that the rift is “profound, substantive, and principled, and compelling all stakeholders to reconsider the path forward”.

The Italian Football Federation (FIGC) initially delayed taking official action but it has now withdrawn its support for Infantino's candidacy in the next Fifa presidential election scheduled.

"This decision is intended to clearly and transparently express the FIGC's position - in full agreement with Uefa and its president Aleksander Ceferin - regarding the recent initiatives promoted by the FIFA president concerning the governance and development of international competitions," FIGC said in a statement.

The Football Association of Montenegro has withdrawn its support for Gianni Infantino’s bid for another term.

“The proposals that have been put forward, as they assessed, have exposed worrying vulnerabilities in terms of governance, not only at the political level but also within the administration,” a statement read. “Therefore, the Football Association of Montenegro has decided to withdraw its letter of support, previously given in favour of Mr Gianni Infantino’s candidacy for re-election in 2027.”

The Royal Dutch Football Association (KNVB) is among the many to have confirmed it has lost confidence in Infantino’s leadership.

“The way this process has unfolded has resulted in a fundamental loss of confidence in the leadership of Fifa President Gianni Infantino. The KNVB no longer has confidence in his leadership,” a statement read.

The Northern Irish football association (IFA) has expressed “concerns” over Infantino’s leadership.

"We take our responsibilities as a member association of both Uefa and Fifa seriously," said an IFA spokesperson. "We support the current Uefa position on the matter and will continue to engage constructively on issues relating to football governance. We are of course aware of the recent developments and wider concerns within the football community. We are considering these matters carefully and will continue to engage through the appropriate governance structures and channels.”

The Norwegian Football Federation (NFF) have been one of the most vocal bodies to call for Infantino’s resignation.

NFF president Lise Klaveness told the Guardian: “We decided that there is no trust in Infantino now. We have been worried consistently and there has been one more step in worrying direction every year, every part of the year. The trust is now gone for us, there is no way back for Infantino.

“We’re left in a very polarised cooperation globally and, I would say, chaos. So we will ask him to resign immediately so that the global football community can look ahead and cooperate going into the future.”

The Football Association of Ireland (FAI) has followed in withdrawing its support for Infantino while backing Uefa’s stance.

"Following an FAI board meeting, the decision was taken to rescind the letter of support that the Association supplied earlier this year," it confirmed. "It has since written to Fifa to explain its reasoning. The Association is grateful for the support that Fifa provides to Irish football, but recent events have raised significant concerns regarding governance, transparency and the lack of meaningful consultation.”

The Romanian Football Federation (FRF) was among the countries to resolutely reject the sell-off proposal, calling it “a dangerous initiative to turn football into a private asset”.

As a result, the FRF will withhold support for Infantino’s re-election for the time being, with a statement reading: “FRF is suspending the discussion regarding the support of Fifa President Gianni Infantino for a new term, until further clarifications.”

The SFA’s chief executive Ian Maxwell told the BBC that the governing body’s position has “been clear” since Uefa’s collective statement.

He said: “We have aligned ourselves with the Uefa position and 55 nations are stronger than one voice. Their statement since then has talked about a lack of confidence in the Fifa leadership and the Scottish FA agree completely with that decision. The SFA will not be voting for Gianni Infantino come the re-election in early 2027.”

The Football Association of Serbia sent a similar letter withdrawing support to Fifa.

“We would like to remind you that we provided our support to Mr Infantino in writing on 25 May of this year, but after carefully considering the events that have seriously damaged the image and reputation of both Fifa and its president in the past period, this is the only logical and rational course of action,” a statement read.

Sweden’s federation (SvFF) followed suit: "At an extraordinary board meeting on Monday, SvFF has chosen not to support Fifa President Gianni Infantino ​in the upcoming re-election.

"This is after a long-term assessment of the development within Fifa and the organization's leadership. The decision is based on recurring shortcomings in governance, transparency and management, which means that trust in Gianni Infantino's leadership no longer exists."

The Swiss Football Association (SFV) withdrew its backing of Infantino’s bid for another term as Fifa president at the end of September, saying it “cannot justify continuing to support Gianni Infantino’s candidacy”.

Their former international captain Ramon Vega is challenging Infantino.

The Football Association of Wales was one of the first to send its letter withdrawing support for Infantino.

“The ‌recent failures in good governance, processes, ‌leadership, values, stakeholder management, communications and sound judgment ​have led us to a position ‌where Mr Infantino has lost the confidence of the FAW to ‌remain at the helm of world football,” a statement read. “Failing to put the best interests of football first is a failure we cannot accept.”

The United States joined forces with Canada to call for “meaningful change that strengthens Fifa’s governance, transparency and accountability”.

Same as above, with Canadian prime minister Mark Carney stating that he no longer has confidence in Infantino.

“It should be fatal,” Carney said following Infantino’s botched sell-off plan. “Certainly I don’t have confidence in Mr. Infantino."

The Japanese Football Federation (JFA) are backing their own candidate for president, with Kozo Tashima challenging Infantino. This signifies a rupture in Asia’s collective backing of Infantino, with Tashima confirming he already has at least five member associations backing him on the continent.

Asked about his ‌objectives, the 68-year-old Tashima said: "First and foremost, I believe it is essential to establish proper governance and ensure that transparency is firmly maintained.

"We must get that right first. Then, on top of that, various policies can be put forward."

Jordan were the first non-Uefa member association to publicly oppose Infantino after federation president Prince Ali bin Hussein levelled accusations of blackmail against Fifa.

Prince Ali alleged the organisation offered assistance with the kingdom's issues in exchange for his endorsement, following Infantino's withdrawal from a World Cup stake sale proposal. Among the problems cited was the association's eight-month wait for prize money, due since December for reaching the Arab Cup final in Qatar.

After taking to X to reveal the outstanding payments, the Jordan Football Association received the prize money two days later - but Prince Ali has not changed his stance over not endorsing Infantino: “It does not change my clear position concerning the leadership of Fifa: my FA and I will not endorse president Infantino, nor will we vote for him.”

New Zealand have broken rank with Oceania’s confederation by withdrawing their support for Infantino.

"After careful consideration, NZF has formed the view that decisions and actions within Fifa have contributed to a breakdown in trust and increased division within international football, and that an independent review is required to restore confidence and trust,” a statement read.

Confederation of African Football (54 members) 🌍

Caf, the governing body for African football, was the first confederation to unanimously back Infantino.

“We are committed to continue working together with Fifa, its member associations, other football confederations and stakeholders to safeguard and adhere to governance, due process, and transparency global best practises and to contribute to the development and growth of football worldwide,” Caf president Patrice Motsepe said.

Motsepe later added that the position of embattled president Infantino can only be decided by Fifa elections.

Asian Football Confederation (47 members) 🌏

The Asian Football Confederation (AFC) has endorsed Infantino in an apparent turnaround in its stance from two months ago when it criticised the head of football’s world governing body for a breach of trust.

AFC president Sheikh Salman bin Ibrahim Al Khalifa, speaking at the body’s extraordinary congress meeting in Tokyo on Friday, embraced Infantino with a large hug in front of the membership as he delivered his endorsement.

“We affirm our full commitment to supporting you and your leadership as you seek a new mandate as the Fifa president.” Sheikh Salman said. “You’ve visited a lot of countries in Asia. I’ve spoken to a lot of members within this continent. I know your feeling and I think your commitment is clear as well.”

South American governing body Conmebol, with its 10 member associations, expressed “concern about the repeated unilateral actions taken” and welcomed the decision to withdraw the plans, affirming it will “not support any action” that “ignores or deviates” from the “democratic procedures that represent the 211 Fifa member associations”.

But having already endorsed Infantino in the summer, it appears they have stuck with their stance and not wavered as one of the Swiss-Italian’s firmest strongholds. Reports state that Conmebol “maintains its support for Infantino”, with an official statement set to be made following its meeting in Barranquilla on 20 November.

Following a pair of visits to the Caribbean, the CFU announced on Friday that it will endorse Infantino for another term. This is despite Concacaf, the governing body of North America and the Caribbean, being in opposition.

“The CFU emphasizes that it determined its collective position through consultation with its member associations,” the organisation said in a statement. “In this same spirit the CFU continues to call for increased transparency, good governance, and meaningful reform within the football family.”

Uefa is not fully unified in its opposition of Infantino, with Azerbaijan one of two nations to publicly back the incumbent president.

“The Association of Football Federations of Azerbaijan (AFFA) fully supports Fifa and Fifa President Gianni Infantino,” a statement read after a visit to Hovsan.

Similarly, the association of Latvia has stood in support of Infantino amid criticism from Uefa.

“Gianni Infantino does so many things that it is difficult to list in one minute, but I am grateful for his support around the world, his contribution to the development of football, and, of course, Latvia is always on his side,” said federation president Vadims Lyashenko. “He can count on our support.”

Benin’s federation looks set to back Infantino in next March’s election.

“I see no reason not to support him,” said Mathurin de Chacus, president of the Benin federation.

FA president Alexandre Muyenge of Burundi echoed the remarks by his Benin counterpart supporting Infantino.

He said: “He is a president who is doing what is necessary to support Africa, particularly the less wealthy countries. His record is good, so it is logical to support him."

The president of the Comoros Football Federation, Said Ali Athouman, backed Infantino’s scheme due to the financial benefits to smaller nations.

"Our problem is how to obtain money,” he said. “Right now, we have no sponsors. So how do we compete? The richest nations can train players, have infrastructure and equipment, and it's no coincidence that they then achieve results. Most European countries don't have ​these problems.”

The Djiboutian Football Federation has similarly welcomed the decision to withdraw the sell-off proposal, with president Souleiman Hassan Waberi saying the climbdown “demonstrates a firm commitment to preserving the unity, solidarity and cohesion that define the football family”.

He said in a statement: “I reaffirm my full support for Fifa President Mr Gianni Infantino and for all initiatives aimed at promoting the sustainable, transparent and inclusive development of football around the world.”

DR Congo's federation (Fecofa) president Veron Mosengo-Omba, who was only elected in May, spoke out in “full support” of Infantino. The two studied at university in Switzerland together.

Fecofa said it had "greatly benefited" from Fifa's programmes, citing investment in development and infrastructure.

Egypt have maintained their endorsement of Infantino, similar to heir North African counterparts.

"The Egyptian Football Association reiterates its full trust in President Gianni Infantino's leadership and its continuous support for any initiatives that benefit Fifa member associations and the future of world football,” a statement read.

Lamin Kaba Bajo told BBC Sport that his federation not just stood by Infantino, but would do "everything within our means" to get him re-elected.

"That's my personal opinion as president of the federation, and a good number of African memberships also stand on that line. He is here to stay,” he said.

Fleetwood Haiya, Malawi's football chief, cited the nation’s financial concerns in why he supported Infantino.

He told BBC Sport: "Our government is not able to make sure that they have medical supplies in hospitals and at the same time to look at football. Malawi needs money."

Ahmed Yahya, the country's representative on the Fifa Council, was one of a number of African football leaders to say he was backing Infantino early on, pledging his support before Caf took its stance.

The location of Infantino’s initial crisis meeting back in the summer, Morocco have stuck by the Swiss-Italian as one of his most loyal allies.

“What has been achieved in recent years through Fifa’s development programmes, particularly in Africa, represents an important accomplishment that must be preserved, to the benefit of all member associations,” said the Royal Moroccan Football Federation’s president Fouzi Lekjaa.

Like Mauritania, Hamidou Djibrilla from Niger came out quickly in support of Infantino.

The Nigeria Football Federation (NFF) has largely aligned itself towards a supportive stance for Infantino's leadership, with former NFF president and deputy chairman of Fifa's Men's National Teams Competitions Committee, Amaju Pinnick, backing his initial sell-off plan.

Namibia Football Association president Robert Shimooshili has backed Infantino and reiterated that his federation stands with Caf.

“If CAF rallies behind Infantino, then we also support the president of Fifa at the moment. We have confidence in him, and we don’t mind what Uefa or AFC or Concacaf say. That is their right and we cannot do anything about it,” he said.

Sudan’s federation joined five other Arab nations - Qatar, Lebanon, Morocco, Egypt and Mauritania - in writing a joint statement backing Infantino.

"Recognising the important role of Arab football in bringing people together and strengthening co-operation among football associations across the Arab world, we ... express our full support for Mr Gianni Infantino, president of Fifa, and reaffirm our confidence in his leadership and continued commitment to the development of football worldwide," the statement read.

"We deeply appreciate Mr Infantino’s sustained efforts to advance football globally, expand opportunities across all regions and strengthen the game’s role in bringing people and communities together. We look forward to continuing our close cooperation with him towards a stronger and more prosperous future for world ‌football, expanding opportunities and further enhancing Arab football’s contribution to ​the global game."

While not an official statement from the federation, South Africa’s Minister of Sports, Arts and Culture of South Africa, Gayton McKenzie, posted on X saying that Infantino had been "very good to African football".

"Africa should be cautious in joining the public lynching of Infantino,” he warned. “He has been very good to African football. We can debate his ideas but let's not forget who has been a friend to us. Many of those calling for his head want the old status quo back too."

The Ugandan federation are said to have echoed the sentiments of Malawi and comoros, with football officials from the federation telling BBC Sport that Fifa investment has transformed their game both on and off the pitch

The Bhutan Football Federation has published a letter that extends its “sincere appreciation” to Infantino for scrapping the plans, hailing him for demonstrating “true leadership” by “prioritising consensus over commerce”.

“We stand united with Fifa and President Infantino in their ongoing efforts to serve the global football community,” wrote federation president Dasho Ugen Tsechup.

Like others, Kuwait welcomed the decision to halt the plans but Sheikh Ahmed Yusuf Al-Sabah, the president of the Kuwait Football Association, affirmed his support for Infantino.

A statement read: “He concluded by affirming the Kuwait Football Association's confidence in the leadership of Fifa President Gianni Infantino, and its support for efforts aimed at developing the game and enhancing its opportunities for growth in a way that serves all national associations and achieves the shared interests of global football.”

Lebanon, who granted Infantino citizenship last year, have backed the Swiss-Italian, with FA president Hashim Haidar all but echoing the same sentiments as Kuwait.

“Haidar reaffirms the Lebanese Football Association’s support for Fifa President Gianni Infantino and his confidence in the decisions he makes in the best interests of global football, as well as in his efforts and initiatives aimed at developing the game,” he said, while also asserting that Infantino’s proposal “could be re-examined ... in a away that serves the greater good of the sport and preserves its unity”.

The president of the Indonesian FA supported Infantino’s sell-off plan and has now backed him for another term.

Hailing Fifa as a “strategic partner that is truly present” in its football development, Erick Thohir said: “This is proof of President Infantino’s vision that football development must not be centered on a single region; it must proceed worldwide.”

The Mongolian Football Federation has likewise put out a statement in support of Infantino.

Qatar was the first football association to publicly back Infantino during his attempts to sell off the World Cup, even though they later welcomed the decision to scrap the plan.

"While the proposal had merit, we applaud the wisdom behind prioritising unity among member associations. The Qatar Football Association fully supports President Infantino's efforts to continue growing and strengthening the game globally,” a statement read.

Philippines have also backed Infantino and called for co-operation among confederations.

"We stand behind President Gianni Infantino's leadership, and trust that continued dialogue among all confederations and member associations will keep the global game moving forward together,” the Philippines confirmed.

Saudi Arabia, the controversial host of the 2034 World Cup, unsurprisingly came out in support of Infantino in the fallout of the scandal.

"At this time, SAFF supports the continued efforts ​of Fifa President Gianni Infantino and Fifa’s leadership to restore unity across ⁠the football family and continue growing the game for the benefit of all," ​the federation said in a statement on its website.

Like Qatar, Sri Lanka welcomed the decision to scrap the investment plan and has since reaffirmed its support for Infantino.

"One of President Infantino's defining leadership qualities has always been his willingness to listen to the views and concerns of Member Associations. This decision is a clear reflection of his inclusive leadership style, built on dialogue, mutual respect, and a genuine commitment to serving the global football community," its statement read.

The UAE Football Association backed Infantino after Fifa withdrew its proposed private investment plan, with president Sheikh Hamdan bin Mubarak Al Nahyan saying the FA “reaffirmed its confidence” in his leadership.

Argentina became the first member from South America to voice its support for Infantino, on the same day that confederation Conmebol - which had previously only expressed backing of Infantino - expressed concern over Fifa’s procedures.

"Of our highest consideration, on behalf of the Argentine Football Association (AFA), and its Executive Committee, we address you, dear President, and through your worthy intermediary the Fifa board, to express our support for the management carried out during the last 10 years, which had as its main axes the development of football throughout the world and institutional solidity based on a clear governance model, stable and transparent,” a statement read.

The Ecuador federation (FEF) welcomed the proposal’s withdrawal and expressed confidence in Infantino’s leadership.

“We trust that you, and those who are currently leading football, will continue working for the benefit of our sport,” read the statement.

Paraguay also followed suit as Infantino’s Conmebol support base surfaced.

Their federation published a letter of support addressed to Infantino, writing that it "reaffirms its commitment to continue working alongside Fifa”.

Venezuela was another from South America to publicly support Infantino.

“We reiterate our commitment to continue working shoulder to shoulder under your leadership and with the other sister associations to promote the massification and excellence of all modalities of our discipline,” read a statement from the federation.

One of the many island nations to break away from Concacaf is Antigua and Barbuda, with the Caribbean being re-established as an Infantino stronghold.

“[Infantino’s] transformative policies, from ground-breaking investment in grassroots infrastructure to the historic expansion of World Cup access, have elevated the aspirations of emerging football nations into attainable realities,” the ABFA said in a statement. “By listening with genuine humility and acting with decisive conviction, he has forged a legacy of inclusivity that transcends the sport itself, affirming that every nation, regardless of size, deserves equal voice and fair opportunity on the world stage.”

Another Caribbean federation to support Infantino’s leadership is the British Virgin Islands.

BVI FA president Avanell Morton said: “Well, what more can he do? He’s doing a fantastic job already and doing a fantastic job for the entire region. It’s about football and as long as you are about football, then you have the opportunity to make football the priority.”

Grenada’s federation (GFA) has also broken away from the Concacaf block and reaffirmed its support for Infantino.

“The GFA reaffirms its support for Fifa President Gianni Infantino and expresses its sincere appreciation for his continued support for football development in Grenada and across the Caribbean over his period of service in office and for the years to come,” a statement read.

JFF president Michael Ricketts has similarly reaffirmed his federation’s support in Infantino, breaking away from the Concacaf stance.

“I think Infantino has done so very well for football globally,” he said. “I remember when I got into football as the president, the support from Fifa was medial to say the least.It has been a lot better now and we are just very grateful and we are looking forward to further improvements so that we can impact not just football but the social well-being of every boy and girl who plays the sport.”

Mexico is the only non-Caribbean Concacaf member to support Infantino, breaking away from the stance taken by its 2026 World Cup co-hosts, the United States and Canada.

The Mexican Football Federation (FMF) stated: "The FMF will neither recognise nor approve any process convened outside of that institutional framework, and it joins the call made... by President Infantino to collectively continue developing football for the benefit of the 211 member associations."

The Saint Kitts and Nevis Football Association (SKNFA) has welcomed Infantino’s decision to scrap the plan, called for respect of Fifa’s institutional processes and backed the president to continue.

It said: “The SKNFA concluded by reaffirming its support for Infantino’s leadership and vision, and said it looks forward to supporting him as he completes his current constitutional mandate and continues efforts to advance football development across Fifa’s 211 Member Associations.”

One of just three Caribbean nations to ever qualify for a World Cup, Trinidad and Tobago president Kieron Edwards says he has no doubts that the TTFA will back Infantino next year.

“We would have given President Infantino our full support and we have not wavered with that. Things do come up, changes, you have to have discussions, you have to have dialogue but the support is there 100 per cent behind the President always,” he said.

With the Oceania Football Confederation yet to take an official stance, the Football Federation of American Samoa to come out on their own and back Infantino, sending a letter to the incumbent president to express “sincere gratitude for your outstanding leadership of the the Fifa family worldwide”.

New Caledonia federation president Steeve Laigle has acknowledged Infantino’s “political mistake” but continues to defend the Fifa chief, whose World Cup expansion allowed the tiny Pacific island to reach a World Cup play-off.

Papua New Guinea’s association (PNGFA) was quick to “reaffirm its full support for Fifa president Gianni Infantino” in the aftermath of his botched privatisation bid.

The Solomon Islands also expressed its “continued confidence in his leadership” in regards to Infantino’s presidency.

Tahiti are also said to have expressed their support before the OFC have taken an official stance.

The Oceania Football Confederation (OFC), with its 11 member associations, stopped short from pulling its support for Infantino in their statement after welcoming the scrapping of the sell-off plan, very much staying on the fence as it called for a “review”.

A statement read: "The OFC recognises the progress achieved over the last decade in advancing football development in Oceania under Fifa's leadership and encourages Fifa to use the review as an opportunity to identify and implement any changes that may be necessary."

Individual members of the confederation have already taken stances, as seen above.

As things stand, The Independent tallies that Infantino has 142 member associations backing him for re-election, while 71 members are outwardly confirmed to be opposing him. This is taking into account the endorsements of confederations, which are being considered as a blanket vote from all members under that governing body unless a member has broken rank.

For example, Jordan’s opposition to Infantino means he is not included in the other 46 AFC member associations supporting Infantino, per the confederation’s endorsement, while the likes of Mexico, Jamaica, Trinidad and Tobago and more are all separate from the other Concacaf members in opposition.

This suggests that Infantino is well on course for re-election. However, each nation has a vote for itself and some could go against their confederation’s stance. Japan’s decision to run a rival candidate signifies Asia is not unanimous with its support of Infantino, with Tashima having at least five other member associations backing him - something he will later reveal. Meanwhile, the Brazilian federation president publicly opposed Infantino’s sell-off plans and may not be aligned with Conmebol.

Nevertheless, there will need to be a massive swing from within Infantino’s strongholds if the tide is to turn against the Swiss-Italian come next March’s election.

FA considered legal challenge to stop Gianni Infantino standing in Fifa election

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The FA decided against the challenge of Infantino’s eligibility after conceding it would not succeed

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

The Football Association considered a legal challenge to Gianni Infantino’s eligibility to stand for a fourth term as Fifa president but decided it would not succeed, its chair Debbie Hewitt has said.

Hewitt, a Fifa vice-president and Fifa Council member, was not on the Council in 2022 when it took a decision to discount Infantino’s first term from 2016 to 2019 from the three-term limit set out in Fifa’s statutes, effectively clearing him to stand again next year.

The FA was asked by human rights group FairSquare’s Reboot campaign to challenge Infantino’s eligibility, but as first reported by the BBC, Hewitt has responded to say the FA had already considered it and decided against it.

“The FA has explored the possibility of challenging Mr Infantino’s eligibility to run for a further term as Fifa president but does not consider that such a challenge would secure the outcome sought,” Hewitt wrote in a letter seen by the Press Association.

“Ultimately the process followed Fifa’s governance and led to an outcome where Mr Infantino’s part term was disregarded for the purposes of maximum term limits. Having considered the process in detail, we do not believe that a challenge would succeed.”

The FA declined to comment on the letter, but it is understood the exploration of a legal challenge to the term limits took place when other efforts to extract information from Fifa about the controversial World Cup private investor plan and the Folarin Balogun disciplinary case had been exhausted.

The FA was the only national association which objected to changes in Uefa’s statutes in 2024 which have now allowed Aleksander Ceferin to stand for a fourth term as its president next year.

Infantino has stated he intends to stand for a fourth term at the Fifa Congress in Morocco in March and appears well placed to win, with the deadline for candidates to be submitted having now passed.

Uefa said in August it had lost confidence in Infantino over the private investor plan and would work to identify a credible candidate to stand against him.

On Monday it was announced French Football Federation president Philippe Diallo had the backing of national associations on the Uefa executive committee and European members of the Fifa Council, including Hewitt.

She intends to travel to Zurich to scrutinise documents related to the World Cup private investor plan, following an offer to do so made by Fifa’s secretary general Mattias Grafstrom on 2 November.

However, that trip is understood to be dependent on the conditions Fifa seek to impose and the terms of any non-disclosure agreements it insists must be signed.

Fifa confirmed on Thursday morning that three candidates had been proposed by the national FAs – Infantino, Diallo and Kohzo Tashima, the honorary president of the Japanese Football Association.

Fifa said the candidates’ names would now be submitted to its electoral committee for eligibility checks, and that national associations would be notified no later than 18 February on the final list of admitted candidates.

FA considered legal challenge to stop Gianni Infantino standing in Fifa election

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Football Association considered a legal challenge to Gianni Infantino's eligibility to stand for a fourth term as Fifa president but decided it would not succeed, its chair Debbie Hewitt has said.

Hewitt, a Fifa vice-president and Fifa Council member, was not on the Council in 2022 when it took a decision to discount Infantino's first term from 2016 to 2019 from the three-term limit set out in Fifa's statutes, effectively clearing him to stand again next year.

The FA was asked by human rights group FairSquare's Reboot campaign to challenge Infantino's eligibility, but as first reported by the BBC, Hewitt has responded to say the FA had already considered it and decided against it.

"The FA has explored the possibility of challenging Mr Infantino's eligibility to run for a further term as Fifa president but does not consider that such a challenge would secure the outcome sought," Hewitt wrote in a letter seen by the Press Association.

"Ultimately the process followed Fifa's governance and led to an outcome where Mr Infantino's part term was disregarded for the purposes of maximum term limits. Having considered the process in detail, we do not believe that a challenge would succeed."

The FA declined to comment on the letter, but it is understood the exploration of a legal challenge to the term limits took place when other efforts to extract information from Fifa about the controversial World Cup private investor plan and the Folarin Balogun disciplinary case had been exhausted.

The FA was the only national association which objected to changes in Uefa's statutes in 2024 which have now allowed Aleksander Ceferin to stand for a fourth term as its president next year.

Infantino has stated he intends to stand for a fourth term at the Fifa Congress in Morocco in March and appears well placed to win, with the deadline for candidates to be submitted having now passed.

Gianni Infantino (right) next to FA CEO Mark Bullingham (PA)

Uefa said in August it had lost confidence in Infantino over the private investor plan and would work to identify a credible candidate to stand against him.

On Monday it was announced French Football Federation president Philippe Diallo had the backing of national associations on the Uefa executive committee and European members of the Fifa Council, including Hewitt.

She intends to travel to Zurich to scrutinise documents related to the World Cup private investor plan, following an offer to do so made by Fifa's secretary general Mattias Grafstrom on 2 November.

However, that trip is understood to be dependent on the conditions Fifa seek to impose and the terms of any non-disclosure agreements it insists must be signed.

Fifa confirmed on Thursday morning that three candidates had been proposed by the national FAs – Infantino, Diallo and Kohzo Tashima, the honorary president of the Japanese Football Association.

Fifa said the candidates' names would now be submitted to its electoral committee for eligibility checks, and that national associations would be notified no later than 18 February on the final list of admitted candidates.

Tzolis on Arsenal’s title quest: It’s a marathon

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Christos Tzolis knows that Arsenal's quest for glory on multiple fronts this season is a marathon, and not a sprint.

Arsenal went into the international break five points clear at the top of the Premier League.

They have suffered just one defeat in the top flight all season, and after a shock loss to Fleetwood Town in the EFL Cup on October 27, the Gunners bounced back by beating Liverpool, Slavia Prague and Hull City.

Tzolis, who joined Arsenal from Club Brugge in the summer, insists Mikel Arteta's team are taking it slow and steady.

"It's like a marathon," he told Arsenal's official website.

"It's not because you win one game that everything is OK, it's a very tricky league. Every team is very good and the quality is very high so you have to be focused and alert for every game.

"Every game counts. Every point counts. That's the most important thing and our target is to be focused and alert 100% of every single game.

"We have a squad of 25-26 players at a very, very high level, I don’t think anyone else has this level, so the only thing is to maintain this mentality and focus."

Next up for the Gunners is a trip to St James' Park to face Newcastle United.

Arteta's next win will be his 159th as a manager in the Premier League. It will take him above Mark Hughes (158) into outright 10th for wins by managers in the competition's history.

"It's having the mentality, even before the game, to go in with the mindset that you're going to win, even if the performance is not that good," Tzolis said when asked what it takes to be a winner.

"It's about feeling that you can always find a solution, always to take the three points, because that's what matters.

"If you go with the right plan and the quality we have, that makes it even easier to win the game. But even when you struggle, it's about having the winning mentality in your mind and having everybody – even the coaching staff – on the same page, to do everything to get the win."

Only Exequiel Palacios (68) has won possession more in the Premier League this season than Hall (67), who leads the division outright for possession won in the defensive third (41), while he has played 10 key passes.

None of Newcastle's last 13 Premier League home games against Arsenal have been drawn (W4 L9), with the Magpies winning three of the last five.

Arsenal have only won more Premier League games against Everton (41) and West Ham (39) than they have against Newcastle (38), while their 32 clean sheets against the Magpies is the most one side has against another in the competition.

Newcastle have won just one of their last 29 Premier League games against sides starting the day top of the table (D8 L20), and none of their last 18 (D4 L14) since a 2-1 victory over Chelsea in December 2014.

The Magpies are also winless in 14 Premier League games against reigning champions (D5 L9) since beating Manchester City 2-1 in January 2019.

The Gunners have won their last four Premier League games by an aggregate score of 5-0. They last won five consecutive league games without conceding a goal in September/October 1987.

Arsenal boss Slegers wary of Lyon’s athletic qualities ahead of showdown

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Renee Slegers warned her Arsenal players that they will come up against an "athletic" Lyon side when they square off in their Champions League encounter on Thursday.

The Mangata Developments Stadium Meadow Park is the venue for the clash between two sides that have yet to taste defeat in this season's competition.

Arsenal are eighth in the 18-team standings with eight points from their opening four fixtures, two fewer than Lyon, who have the opportunity to top the table with a victory.

Slegers' side come into the match following a 1-1 draw with London City Lionesses in the Women's Super League, while Lyon have nine wins from nine in the Premiere Ligue, keeping them five points clear of Paris Saint-Germain.

This will be the 12th meeting between Arsenal and Lyon in the Champions League, with the French side winning seven of the previous 11 encounters (D1 L3).

Indeed, their number of victories are the most against the Gunners in the competition, and Slegers acknowledged the difficulties that await her team.

"They are three different teams, so Barcelona work with very close relationships, close connections on the pitch," Slegers said.

"London City was a little different in that sense, but there were similarities. What they do similarly is very high and aggressive pressure, jump pressure.

"Lyon are a very athletic team as well, they have a lot of fast players, so we'll have to manage that really well and defend that really well, and make sure that we're in good areas on the pitch and have numbers around the ball.

Dortmund's Ricken: Nmecha didn't intend to disrespect with celebration

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Borussia Dortmund managing director for sports Lars Ricken said that he held conversations with Felix Nmecha over a controversial goal celebration and that the midfielder stressed that he didn't intend to be disrespectful with his gesture.

"We truly respect his Christian faith. He has made it clear to us that he did not intend for his gesture to be disrespectful, discriminatory, or even proselytizing, and that's how he's lived since his first day at Borussia Dortmund," Ricken told broadcasters Sky.

He emphasized "how much we value Felix as a player and as a person." and explained they had "truly had a trusting, internal conversation" with the Germany national team player. "And we want to leave it at that and please get back to focusing on the on-field issues."

Nmecha's recurring goal celebration, in which he takes an imaginary crown from his head and places it on the pitch in front of him while kneeling, has been heavily criticized, including by Dortmund's fan department.

He explained that the celebrations is about "putting down my 'crown' in front of the soon-to-come king and point out to Jesus, my hope and my strength."

The gesture is used by the network Ballers in God, which has been criticized for homophobic and queerphobic statements made by its members.

Ballers in God is a network of Christian footballers founded in 2015 that records and shares many such celebrations on social media.

Dortmund president Hans-Joachim Watzke said last week that he asked Nmecha to refrain from doing the celebration in the future.

Nick Wright says he’s ‘very surprised’ after hearing the mistake the Minnesota Vikings are about to make

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Minnesota Vikings are set to take on the San Francisco 49ers in Mexico City during Week 11 of the 2026/27 NFL season.

The Vikings have a 7-2 record this year and have recently reinforced their quarterback position with Kirk Cousins, who had a key role in their win over the Green Bay Packers last time out.

As for the 49ers, they are 8-1 this term and on paper, this should be a close game. But Nick Wright has now highlighted the impact of the altitude both teams will have to contend with this weekend.

When doing so, he mentioned the Niners 'mitigating this as best they can by practising in Colorado Springs'. But he then went on to say he's 'very surprised' that the Vikings are not doing some similar altitude training ahead of the game, suggesting they should maybe 'go to a place in Denver' to train.

Speaking on First Things First, he said: "Altitude has a massive impact. We just came off the World Cup, where we talked about how it was almost unfair that Mexico were going to play England at Estadio Azteca. Now, England ended up winning that game, but Mexico had suffered their third defeat in their last 90 games there. A huge part of that is that it's one of the most adverse environments in which to play sports.

"We talk a ton about the Nuggets' home-court advantage. You talk about the Broncos having won 18 of 19 in their building.

"So, I do think the Niners mitigating this as best they can by practising in Colorado Springs is the only reason I'm not calling it brilliant. It's almost obvious. Like, yes, clearly this is what they should do.

"And if I were the Vikings, I'm very surprised they're not finding a similar type of environment. I don't know if you said they're at the Air Force Academy. Maybe you have to go to a place in Denver or somewhere where you could do it as well.

"So, I do think this could have a massive impact on who wins this game. I think it'll be a close, good game, and I expect the Niners to be far better in the second half than Minnesota. And it could also impact the number one seed."

The 49ers have the best record in the NFC ahead of Week 11, while the Vikings are joint-second alongside the Detroit Lions and the Chicago Bears.

San Francisco also have previous experience playing in Mexico, having beaten the Arizona Cardinals 38-10 back in 2022.

As for the Vikings, this will be their first-ever NFL game in the country.

Nick Sirianni delivers glowing Aaron Rodgers verdict ahead of Steelers showdown

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Nick Sirianni has shared his admiration for Aaron Rodgers ahead of the Philadelphia Eagles' clash with the Pittsburgh Steelers, praising the veteran quarterback's longevity and ability to make things happen on the field.

The meeting is a rare regular-season Keystone State showdown, with Rodgers now playing under former Green Bay Packers head coach Mike McCarthy in Pittsburgh.

The Steelers entered the Week 11 contest with a 6-3 record, while Rodgers is approaching the end of a career that has spanned more than two decades.

Speaking to reporters after Philadelphia's bye week, Sirianni explained why he has so much respect for Rodgers and the challenge he presents.

He said: "Man, he's been such a good player for such a long time. I have so much respect for him and the success that he's had over decades of football. People tell me I look like him and we look alike. I've actually [had] someone ask me at a restaurant one time, 'Man, that's Aaron Rodgers.' But I've got a lot of respect for him. Man, he's so fun to watch throw the football and how it comes off his hands, how he's able to get himself out of trouble, and how he's able to direct the offense up there."

Sirianni also praised McCarthy, who reunited with Rodgers in Pittsburgh after their successful spell together in Green Bay.

He added: "Same thing with [Pittsburgh Steelers Head] Coach [Mike] McCarthy. I have so much respect for him. As you look at Coach McCarthy, all he's ever done is win everywhere he's been. I mean, the guy's a winner, and together they're winners. It's a good match that they've had for a long time, and it's kind of cool that they can rekindle that. But I have so much respect for the Pittsburgh Steelers organization as a whole, particularly those two guys who are leading the way there."

Rodgers and McCarthy enjoyed one of the defining quarterback-coach partnerships of the 2010s, reaching the playoffs in eight consecutive seasons from 2009 to 2016.

Their time together also produced a Super Bowl victory in 2010.

With Rodgers approaching his 43rd birthday and his career nearing its conclusion, Pittsburgh will hope the reunion can deliver another championship. The Steelers are still chasing their first Super Bowl title since the 2008 season, making the Rodgers-McCarthy partnership particularly important.

Ecuadorian footballer banned for eight years for throwing rock at referee

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Ecuador’s federation says it will go to Fifa to try and make the ban worldwide

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

An eight-year ban has been handed to Ecuadorian footballer Jhon Perlaza after he threw a stone that hit an assistant referee during a third-tier match.

The game, between Perlaza's side Club Ciudad de Yantzaza and Club Rio Aguarico FC, took place on 17 October.

The rock was thrown in the 96th minute and struck assistant referee Lenin Giovanni Ojeda Nunez, after which the match was abandoned.

Ecuador's football federation (FEF) announced the sanction in a statement on Wednesday.

"The assistant referee suffered serious injuries that required surgery and a 60-day recovery period," the FEF said in a statement on Wednesday.

"The disciplinary committee found the player responsible for physical aggression against a match official and suspended him for eight years from all football-related activity, until 19 October 2034.

"In addition, the FEF will ask Fifa to extend this sanction worldwide."

The suspension runs from the federation's disciplinary process, which held the player responsible for physical aggression against a match official.

Yantzaza were also punished by the FEF, which said the club must pay a fine of $2,000 (£1520.19).

A further $4,369.73 (£3,321.41) is owed to cover the assistant referee's medical expenses.

The club issued its own statement on 20 October, apologising to the refereeing team and the fans for what happened.

"As an institution, we categorically reject all acts of aggression, whether verbal or ‌physical, both inside and outside sporting venues. ​We firmly believe that football should be ‌a space of respect, ⁠coexistence, and fair play," Yantzaza said.

"Once the person ⁠responsible for this regrettable incident was identified, the institution made the ‌decision to ​remove him immediately, and he ‌is therefore no longer ​part of our club."

The unthinkable has come to pass: Tom Brady is a pretty good broadcaster

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

I have accidentally force-fed myself Tom Brady's commentary as my primary means of watching the NFL this season. When my favorite team, the Steelers, hired Mike McCarthy as head coach, I lodged a silent protest by declining to spend the several hundred dollars needed to get NFL Sunday Ticket. I didn't even splurge for RedZone. That means I've found myself watching Fox's NFL broadcasts on a Sunday – often an underwhelming Cowboys game – where Brady is the color commentator in the network's No 1 booth, alongside play-by-play man Kevin Burkhardt.

This two-and-a-half-month experiment has led me to an alarming conclusion, one I'd seen others reaching gradually over the last year but never taken too seriously myself. Recently, though, the truth has become unavoidable: Tom Brady is pretty good at his job. Gone are the days of the best quarterback of all time being hopelessly outmatched on TV. What has changed is that Brady now sounds like an actual human being.

Brady's early struggles in his current job, which he started in 2024, are the stuff of broadcast legend – a way to remind people of how humbling a new gig can be even for a talented, beautiful superstar. At the beginning of his run, Brady had a penchant for waiting too long to deliver points that were fine but not especially insightful for a man whose brain is a football supercomputer. The pregnant pause followed by an observation like "they are bringing all six, and they can only block five of them" was typical.

Related: Everybody hates Pat: is a cabal of college kids really trying to bring down ESPN's star meathead?

At times, the legendary quarterback used the Fox broadcast to write his own hagiography. "The people that mattered the most to me were the guys in the locker room," he said one week, throwing shade on quarterback Daniel Jones, who had recently asked the Giants to release him after he was benched. "I showed up every day. I don't care if they asked me to be scout-team safety, be scout-team quarterback. I was going to do whatever I could to help the team win."

But this kind of stuff has gotten less frequent over the past two years. On Sunday, as he called the 49ers' frenetic win over, yes, the Cowboys, Brady showed why Fox gave him a 10-year, $375m deal to do this job. (OK, so technically, Fox gave him the deal to be a corporate ambassador, to help lock in sponsors, and to show the NFL how seriously it takes its product. But anyway …) Brady conveyed some of the uncontrollable joy for the game that once made Tony Romo a good color commentator, but he also explained the sport in ways most other analysts would not.

A good color commentator needs to add value on the simple stuff, as well as make sense of the complexities of the game. In the 49ers-Cowboys game, Brady did both tasks well. When Christian McCaffrey scored on a 66-yard catch and run in the fourth quarter, Brady quickly telestrated the play and showed how Dallas's pressure package left McCaffrey in a perfect position to run an angle route against Von Miller, who is known for rushing the QB rather than for his ability to cover a player as elusive as McCaffrey.

"He's one-on-one with Von Miller because they're pressuring on the inside," Brady said. "That's not the assignment Von Miller's looking for." Indeed it was not, and the understatement was perfect. Anyone could see from the skycam behind the play how Dallas's decision to put Miller in coverage broke down.

Earlier, 49ers receiver Deebo Samuel had scored on a 70-yard reverse. Brady delivered an advanced dissertation on the touchdown in about 25 seconds. With the camera panned out to the all-22 angle from high above the stadium, he circled Samuel with his "nice telestrator" and paused the play at the perfect moment to show how San Francisco had used misdirection to almost completely seal off the Dallas defense by the time Samuel was getting the ball. "They basically eliminated 10 of the 11 defenders," Brady said, paving the way for a "red wave." When the play was actually going on, Brady even emitted an endearing shriek as his fellow quarterback, Brock Purdy, threw a block to help Samuel score.

Brady has also made himself, dare I say, likable? In his playing days, it became NFL canon that laying a finger on Brady was cause for a roughing-the-passer flag and possibly lifetime imprisonment by league authorities. So it was refreshing when Brady reacted to a ticky-tack personal foul on 49ers safety Malik Mustapha with schematic expertise and a desire to let boys be boys.

"When you play this coverage, that is what the safety position is supposed to do on any throws there. That's the risk-reward of throwing there," Brady said. He played off Burkhardt, who noted that it looked vicious in real time. "It all sounds that way," Brady said, and as the viewer, you know that he'd know. Brady explained a safety's responsibility in cover-two and came across as an advocate for the reasonable fan.

Brady still has some of the roughness around the edges that we saw early in his broadcasting career. In his detailed explanation of Samuel's reverse TD, he briefly got hung up on a word that sounded like it was supposed to be "presence" and then aborted an entire sentence before moving on to the next thing. But combined with his audible excitement at every big play, Brady (who it's worth noting is an extremely rich man with more media training than almost any human alive) comes off as a guy who isn't trying too hard and just really likes telling you about football. That's important: if a guy being paid to talk about the game can't convince you he's excited to be there, why should viewers at home care?

The success isn't entirely surprising. A man who was pathologically dedicated to becoming the most effective quarterback of all time was never going to enjoy being seen as a failure as a broadcaster. No doubt he was up every night crunching tape of John Madden broadcasts throughout the offseason. And, in some ways, Brady's improvement is unfortunate. His early struggles were a fun reminder that not even he could have it all. But midway through his third year on the job, Brady is, against all odds, one of the most stable color guys in football.

Troy Aikman is still a professional but is dealing with the disintegration of his relationship with Joe Buck and ESPN. Romo at CBS is off the job after one of the world's most expensive OWI arrests. Replacement JJ Watt is clearly sharp but also still finding his chemistry with Jim Nantz. Could it really be that the only color commentator in a more authoritative spot right now than Brady is Sunday Night Football's Cris Collinsworth? That's what media critics get for having once counted out Touchdown Tom.

Chelsea have made Bournemouth midfielder Alex Scott their priority target for January as Xabi Alonso looks to strengthen a midfield still adjusting to the departure of Enzo Fernández.

The Blues tried hard to sign Scott during the summer, seeing a £64million bid rejected, but they have refused to abandon their interest.

With Chelsea financially well placed to return with an improved proposal, the 23-year-old could become one of the biggest names to watch when the winter window opens.

According to Daily Briefing, Chelsea’s number-one January target is Scott, with Bournemouth’s reluctance to lose such an important player representing the biggest obstacle.

Significantly, CaughtOffside reports that Chelsea are optimistic after holding talks with Bournemouth this week, while personal terms with the England international are already largely understood to be in place.

A fee around £90m has been discussed, although Bournemouth have previously valued Scott closer to £100m.

Scott’s reputation has risen considerably during his time on the south coast. Pep Guardiola previously described him as an “unbelievable player”, praise highlighted in this Sky Sports profile.

Chelsea are also exploring other areas, with Fermín López, Jules Koundé, Daniel Banjaqui and Maxi Araújo among players being monitored.

Chelsea’s wider recruitment plans are exciting, but Scott arguably makes more immediate sense than another expensive overseas gamble.

He already understands the Premier League, can operate as a No.6, No.8 or No.10 and combines technical quality with the intensity Alonso demands from his midfielders.

Roméo Lavia’s return from injury undoubtedly reduces some urgency, but relying on his fitness would be risky given his recent history. Scott would provide another long-term option alongside Moisés Caicedo while also replacing some of the energy and progression Chelsea lost with Fernández.

The main concern is price. Paying around £90m-£100m is enormous for a 23-year-old midfielder, particularly when Chelsea also want defensive reinforcements.

But Scott fits their strategy better than most alternatives: young, Premier League-proven and still capable of significant improvement.

If Bournemouth are finally prepared to negotiate, Chelsea should prioritise completing this deal rather than spreading January funds too thinly across several ambitious targets.
