Expert: Dan James could 'push' to leave Leeds United - 'He is not happy'

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Daniel James could push for a move away from Leeds United in the January transfer window if his playing time does not improve.

The 29-year-old has dropped out of Daniel Farke's regular starting XI since the season began, and has recently expressed frustrations about his bit-part role in the side.

While he is still seen as a valuable asset by the Whites, given the role he can play off the bench, there are doubts about whether he is of the level required to start regularly.

Former Man United chief scout Mick Brown told Football Insider that James' concerns will only grow ahead of the window, and he could push for an escape route.

The Wales international is keen to be starting games regularly at this stage of his career, and despite still having a part to play at Leeds, could look for an opportunity elsewhere.

A number of clubs are believed to be keeping tabs on his situation, but much will depend on James and whether he can be convinced to remain in his decreased role.

Football Insider revealed last month that Farke is keen to keep James at Elland Road and views him as an important part of his plans, even if he is not starting games.

The winger has featured just six times across all competitions this season, and has started just three times, with the latest of those coming in the 2-0 defeat against Bournemouth.

Should he not return to the starting XI in the near future, for Premier League games rather than in the cup, there is a growing chance he could seek a move away.

"Daniel James is in a bit of a strange position at Leeds, it's hard to know where he stands," Brown told Football Insider.

"He is a valuable asset to have coming off the bench, because he's quick, he's direct, he can put defenders under pressure, and he's good to have when you're chasing a game.

"But despite all of those things I've said, notice there's nothing there about his quality as a player, and that's where the doubts are.

"Physically, he's great, he offers a lot in that department, but his actual skill level has always been in doubt and I'm still not convinced he's a Premier League player in that respect.

"He's done well at Leeds, he's played an important role for them, but now he's not starting games and it looks like they're moving to a level where he won't be needed.

"From his point of view, he is not happy with his role, he wants to be starting games at this stage of his career, and he doesn't just want to be sitting on the bench and coming on every now and then.

"So there is every chance that, if things don't chance soon, he could push for a move away because that's what it might take for him to move on."

Should James be allowed to leave Leeds in the January transfer window, there will be no shortage of suitors, with clubs keeping a close eye on his situation at Elland Road.

Despite his recent struggles and lack of game time, he has attributes which could be valuable for a number of clubs, and they could be tempted to make a move as a result.

Brown told Football Insider last month that James is being eyed by a number of Championship clubs, with a return to the second tier now firmly on the cards.

A number of promotion-hunting sides are believed to be keeping tabs on the Wales international's situation, and could make a move to lure him away when the window opens.

This comes amid a feeling that James is not good enough to play regularly in the Premier League, with a move to the second tier seen as a good opportunity.

Martin Odegaard: Norwegian media are right about Arsenal captain after what did in Denmark clash

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Martin Odegaard put in one of his best-ever Norway performances against Denmark as he impresses at Arsenal this season.

Odegaard has returned to top form so far this season after a tough 2025-26 campaign from an individual standpoint.

He only registered eight combined goals and assists in 36 appearances for the club last term, but already has six in 17 games in 2026-27.

Odegaard has continued his strong start to the campaign over the last week with a starring role in Norway's successive wins over Wales and Denmark.

The national media rightly heaped huge praise on him after his outstanding performance in Tuesday's 3-2 Nations League win over their Scandinavian rivals.

Odegaard will be loving his football right now and looks set to keep impressing as Arsenal return to action against Newcastle this weekend.

Odegaard captained his country to key wins in the latest international break as they clinched second place in their Nations League A group on a head-to-head basis.

The 27-year-old set his country on the comeback trail from 2-0 down by dispossessing Mads Hermansen to set Erling Haaland up for a tap-in, then scored their equaliser with a low shot that crept under the Danish stopper.

He had the most touches (107) and completed the most passes (76) of any player, while he also completed 100 per cent of his dribbles (2/2), made six ball recoveries and won four ground duels.

Norwegian media rightly went crazy for his performance, as VG wrote: "Odegaard delivered one of his absolute best international matches at Parken. The incredible performance came just days after he had been struggling with illness before the Wales match. In addition to goals and assists, the 27-year-old delivered a series of technical feats that left the crowd in awe, including two nutmegs."

While 433.no said: "One of the captain's best international matches. Completely dominated the game on the pitch, deep and high."

Arsenal are moving to tie down a number of their key players to fresh deals right now, with recent reports around impending extensions for David Raya, Jurrien Timber, Declan Rice and Piero Hincapie.

Odegaard has improved massively this season, but is out of contract in 2028, and sources have revealed to Football Insider that Arsenal are also keen to get him tied down to a new deal as Bayern Munich circle.

Speaking about his situation to Norwegian press earlier this week, he said: "I’m happy and hope I can stay there [at Arsenal] for a long time to come, but we haven’t started on that [contract talks] yet."

Ole Gunnar Solsjkaer has 'tipped off Man United' about signing Norwegian sensation - Insider

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester United's connections mean they cannot be ruled out in the race for RB Leipzig sensation Antonio Nusa.

That is according to experienced former Tottenham, Aston Villa and Everton scout and expert on Norwegian football, Bryan King, speaking exclusively to Football Insider.

Several Premier League giants are thought to be in the mix for the 21-year-old, who has been in stunning form for the German club this season.

Nusa has scored four goals and provided seven assists in just nine Bundesliga games, and found the net in a Norway shirt brilliantly when he scored against Wales last week.

The former Club Brugge forward is under contract at Leipzig until 2029, and is expected to depart within the next two transfer windows if they receive a sizable fee.

Man United are expected to be busy in the January window following the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino, and are certainly in need of an addition on the wing.

Unplayable Antonio Nusa 🪄#NationsLeague pic.twitter.com/HWP8BlBCnc

If Man United are to sign Nusa, the move will almost certainly have to be made in the January window.

Several more clubs will join the race in the summer and will be able to offer a far more stable environment for development than the one at Old Trafford.

Nusa's former teammate, Benjamin Sesko, has struggled following his £74million move to Man United in 2025.

"Don't rule Man United out. I've heard that Man United are looking at Nusa," King told Football Insider.

"You've got players here who played for Man United, and if Man United had any sense, they would have contacted Ronny Johnsen or Henning Berg or Ole Gunnar Solskjaer and had an update on this player.

"From what I've heard Ole and the ex-Man United players have already tipped United off.

"I wouldn't rule Liverpool out either. He will go for big money, this boy. He's a good boy. His next move will be big and it'll be Premier League."

Arsenal are expected to be the front runners to sign Nusa, and it is easy to see why he would take the move.

The Gunners are the Premier League champions and current leaders, who, despite having arguably Europe's strongest squad, have a superstar-sized hole on the left of their attack.

Man United will struggle to compete with that, but their sheer size and world-wide name means they can never be ruled out.

Pochettino can offer Nusa the privilege of being the face of the project that attempts to revive the Red Devils, and that will be no easy offer to turn down.

Ranking the 10 most expensive teenage Real Madrid signings ahead of JJ Gabriel move

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

JJ Gabriel’s prolonged move from Manchester United to Real Madrid looks close to being completed, but the much-hyped teenager is not the first of his age group to head to the Spanish capital.

For a club that usually buys ready-to-go superstars, Real Madrid has a surprisingly deep history of signing teenagers, often picking the best talents from across the globe.

It hasn’t always been a success though, with the likes of Martin Odegaard never really settling in, but while the now Arsenal captain was at least cheap, there have been other expensive failures.

Here are the 10 most expensive teenagers signed in Real Madrid’s history, ranked on their impact for the club.

Madrid paid €30m for the opportunity to never play Reinier.

He arrived in January 2020 from Flamengo and was immediately put in the reserve squad.

For the start of the following season, he was loaned out to Dortmund, where he stayed for two years but managed just one league goal in his time.

A return to Spain saw him move to Girona, where he managed two goals in half a season, and he spent the second part of that campaign at Frosinone in Serie A.

His final loan stint came at Granada, where he was equally poor, and Madrid cut their losses, allowing his contract to expire. He is now playing for Atletico Mineiro in Brazil.

Madrid spent big money to entice Hernandez from their city rivals Atletico, but the full-back never really made an impact at the Bernabeu.

He only played 23 matches, of which just 13 were in the league, and was second fiddle to Marcelo.

He found more success elsewhere, particularly at Milan, but it did not work out at Madrid.

Mastantuono was given a chance in the Madrid first team last season, but it looks like he failed to impress those in charge, as he has been sent on loan to Fiorentina.

Madrid spent €45m to bring him to the club from River Plate and put the 18-year-old straight into the first team, allowing him to make 23 La Liga appearances.

He managed just a single goal in that time, but he is still only 19, so could well resurrect his Madrid career after his loan away.

Of Madrid’s teenage attacking talents, Endrick is the one who has not really made an impact.

Breaking into a frontline that consisted of Vinicius Junior, Rodrygo and a newly arrived Kylian Mbappe was always going to be tough, but Endrick did not make the most of his limited opportunities.

He featured in 22 La Liga matches in the 2024/25 season but managed just one goal. He also scored only one in nine Champions League games.

His best form came in the Copa del Rey, with five goals in six games, but Madrid allowed him to go out on loan last winter when he moved to Lyon.

The Spanish giants clearly still believe in him, as they refused to let him leave permanently, but the Brazilian needs to prove that was the correct decision.

The 5 players Real Madrid let go in the summer – & how they’re faring elsewhere

Solid would be a fair way to describe Camavinga at Madrid.

He is the team’s ultimate utility man and has been asked to play left-back, defensive midfielder and central midfielder. Perhaps that flexibility has been why he has struggled to nail down a starting spot.

He has made 11 league appearances this season, of which just two were starts, and his time on the pitch each game is barely more than 10 minutes long.

Still, he’s a decent squad player to have.

Arda Guler is just a fun footballer to watch.

Madrid paid the relatively cheap €30m to bring that fun to the club in 2023 and while it has taken him a while to settle in, he is now one of the most important members of the team.

His first year was disrupted by injuries and it was not until the 2025/26 season that he started to look like a Madrid player.

This year, he has once again gone up a level and provided three goals and four assists in his 13 La Liga matches.

And if you haven’t seen his goal for Turkey recently, treat yourself.

€125m is clearly a lot of money, but the early signs are good for Diomande.

He has eight goal involvements in his 16 games so far, and a timely injury to Vinicius Junior has provided him with a platform on which to shine.

He’s only just turned 20, but there is real talk in Madrid over who is now the starting left winger.

He goes under the radar considering the other attacking talent Madrid have had, but Rodrygo has been a very good signing.

He has scored a steady amount of goals since becoming a first-team regular in 2021 and can play anywhere across the frontline.

That versatility and ability has been missed since he suffered a cruciate ligament tear in March.

It’s hard to judge just where Vinicius Junior belongs on this list.

He was absolutely brilliant for a long time, but there has been a definite drop-off this season. The fact he is producing that form while being paid €600k a week will not go unnoticed.

Before writing off his career as a whole, he has been a clear success. 129 goals and 103 assists in 387 games, including 37 match-winners, show the impact he has had.

His best moment came in 2022 when he scored the only goal of the game as Madrid beat Liverpool in the Champions League final.

It was an easy pick for number one. There may be other more talented players on this list, but Madrid would pay €27m for that goal in Lisbon alone.

When Madrid needed digging out of a hole, it was often Ramos that provided it. During his Madrid career, he scored seven goals beyond the 90-minute mark. A reminder that he was supposed to be a centre-back.

Away from the last-minute heroics, Ramos was the captain of one of the most successful periods in Madrid’s history. Sure, he loved a red card, but for every one of those, you would get 20 excellent performances.

He is not just one of Madrid’s best teenage purchases, but one of their best purchases full stop.

Report: Liverpool set transfer strategy to sign £45m star

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have been linked with a lot of players recently.

The Anfield outfit are massively underperforming with fans demanding that replacements for a decent chunk of their players are sourced in the upcoming transfer windows.

Long-term links with Alex Scott and Adam Wharton have remained while new rumours with players such as Arthur Dias have now started to emerge.

However, before the January transfer window even opens, there are a couple of deals that already feel close to completion.

And now, the Reds seem to have created a transfer strategy to get one deal done.

Liverpool want to negotiate Ronald Araujo's price down before signing him

Right now, Ronald Araujo is probably Liverpool's best-performing summer signing.

The Uruguayan came to Anfield on a season-long loan with a €55 million option to buy.

And, even though he's been one of their best defenders, his injury history and age are a cause for concern.

Whether he's been used as a right-back or a centre-back, he's always done well and has certainly helped solidify Liverpool's back line.

At the moment, Liverpool are more than pleased with Araujo's contributions but, according to an update from Diario Sport, they're not willing to sign him permanently unless they can knock his price down a bit.

"Liverpool want to keep Ronald Araújo, but not at any price," they wrote.

"The English club is very pleased with the Uruguayan center-back's performance, on loan from Barça until the end of the season, although they don't intend to automatically exercise the €55 million purchase option agreed between the two clubs. Their aim is to negotiate a lower fee.

"Liverpool's strategy is to wait until the end of the season to sit down and negotiate with Barça. The Anfield management considers €55 million too high and are confident they can reduce it considerably.

"Liverpool intends to leverage the player's contract situation and high salary to put pressure on Barcelona in negotiations. Barcelona will have to decide whether to accept a lower fee than initially agreed or bring back a player who is proving his worth in the Premier League."

Liverpool signing Ronald Araujo for cheaper than €55 million would be ideal

As mentioned, Araujo has been in fine form this term.

The defender has been a revelation at Anfield but, the €55 million fee is obviously quite steep.

So, if Liverpool manage to knock the price of the defender down and Barcelona agree to sell him, they'll be in the money.

The fact that Araujo can cover both positions is one of his main selling points with versatility being a key part of football in this day an age.

Obviously, the fact that he's got plenty of Champions League experience and will have a year of Premier League football under his belt is also a major selling point.

There's no mention of how much Liverpool would want to pay for Araujo but, if it's anywhere near €35 million - €40 million, it would be an absolute steal.

The best European strikers this century - ranked by you

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The top 10 European strikers of the 21st century

Classic number nines, penalty-box poachers, troublesome targetmen and tireless all-rounders.

There is no one-size fits all when it comes to determining great strikers, but they do all share a common trait - a ruthlessness in front of goal.

This week, however, we put the power in your hands to decide the best European frontmen of the 21st century.

Tens of thousands of you voted on the 20-man shortlist drawn up by our panel.

We then asked European football experts Guillem Balague, Julien Laurens and Raphael Honigstein to discuss your choices on the Euro Leagues podcast.

So from 10-1, here are the best European strikers this century - as voted for by you.

Top 10 European Strikers of the Century - Part One

Karim Benzema comes in at number 10, the former Lyon and Real Madrid frontman's closest challengers coming from across the continent in the shape of Raul, Robin van Persie, Michael Owen and Miroslav Klose.

Laurens: "From a young age you could see he was something special. I know it was difficult at the beginning at Real Madrid, but in the post-Cristiano era he carried that team, wore the armband and won big titles and trophies by being decisive in big moments. Like I used to say 'Karim the dream'."

Honigstein: "The selflessness, the ability to work for the team. A sense of elegance and humility on the pitch, which is unusual in a goalscorer. I think he is unusual in that list. I think not many will define themselves through their teamwork and assists as much as their goals, but he was that player."

Ruud van Nistelrooy edges Benzema, with whom he briefly overlapped at Real Madrid - the Dutchman teed up the Frenchman and scored himself in their only La Liga appearance together, before he left for Hamburg in January 2010 following injury problems.

The former PSV striker's most consistent spell, of course, came at Manchester United, where he scored 150 goals.

Honigstein: "He is the nicest guy but he never came across that way on the pitch. Typical trait of a striker, he would kill to score the goal. I think anyone who has grown up with him scoring goals, for United especially, must have a soft spot for him because he was a phenomenal poacher."

Laurens: "He was a strong player physically, and despite the lack of pace the awareness in the box was just very special even with all the injuries."

Zlatan Ibrahimovic slots in at eight, comfortably clear of Van Nistelrooy and Benzema. The Swede netted more than 500 times in a career that spanned Malmo, Ajax, Juventus, both Milans, Barcelona, Paris St-Germain, Manchester United and LA Galaxy.

Balague: "He was a big-game player and that explains the league titles with PSG. Scoring four goals against England, the overhead kick. The personality thing has put him one or two places in this list, saying 'I am great, I am the best'. If you keep saying you're great it makes people stop and think."

Laurens: "The mentality of really believing he was the best and nobody was better than him, apart from maybe Brazilian Ronaldo, who was his idol growing up, and he would watch videos of him and try to replicate in his council estate. If you watch a highlight reel of his best goals he was incredible."

Honigstein: "What makes him unique among the strikers is his technique, You could see elements of martial arts in his goals. No-one scores goals like that."

The first Englishman on the list, although the third to have represented Manchester United - it's the Old Trafford outfit's record goalscorer, Wayne Rooney.

Honigstein: "I don't think there is anyone who would've watched English football at that time [early in his career] and not been enthralled by his game. The only downside is the combination of injuries and not the most professional lifestyle meant he did not fulfil his potential. He could've been Messi."

Balague: "What could have been, yes. He was the national team's record scorer too before Harry Kane took it. I find him to deserve this place as he had to adapt to a lot of strikers and he did it well. He had the ability to link up and score. He was very intelligent."

Laurens: "He is the most street footballer England has ever seen. There's no rules, that kind of anger you saw on the pitch. I don't think people in England realise how good he was."

What does becoming the all-time World Cup top goalscorer get you? Sixth place on the best European strikers this century list. The Real Madrid forward, 27, just misses out on the top five.

Laurens: "Maybe if we do this ranking again in a few years he will be higher. I would have had him top five, for sure. He has scored more than 400 goals. I don't think Messi and Ronaldo had scored that many at that age, so he's ahead. I don't know if he will play for as long as them but he hasn't done much wrong in his career."

Balague: "This list has to do what you feel about the player, not science - 22 goals in 22 appearances in World Cups and [Euro] Championships. He should be at least top three because of that. Great stats, but we're not in love with him."

Honigstein: "I don't think there is a forward that is as good as footballer as him in 2026. On that basis alone he should be higher, but people when they vote have other things in their head. The only way to win everyone over is to score more goals and win more World Cups for France, which I think is possible."

There was little between Mbappe and Erling Haaland, but it is Manchester City's prolific Norwegian who slips in at fifth. The 26-year-old has already scored more than 300 club goals and has a staggering 68 in 61 games for his country.

Balague: "Mbappe should be higher than him. Haaland scored 52 goals in 53 games [in 2022-23], that is astonishing, but he's gone 11 finals without scoring. We're talking about the best. We're talking about what we see at the moment and what we see is someone who is calculated but in a more charming way. An ability to score we haven't seen in a while; power, ability to finish. He is not world class to the level of others."

Honigstein: "I don't think it's fair to judge him on just that. They [Manchester City] won the Champions League when he was there. When Haaland arrived with Pep Guardiola, they found a way to do it. What makes him unique is the physical attributes and the pace. To have both is unique. He can sprint like a 100m sprinter. Is he the fifth best striker of the 21st Century? I don't think he's that far behind."

Interesting, this, because Robert Lewandowski is well clear of Haaland in fourth, but the leap from the Polish forward to the top three is massive - more than 10,000 votes.

The 38-year-old is still chipping away for Chicago Fire in Major League Soccer, having been a reliably ruthless assassin for Barcelona, Bayern and Dortmund.

Honigstein: "He used to play basketball, judo, handball - his father wanted him to have a wholesome education. You could see his completeness that he had as a centre-forward. Nothing in his game was extraordinary but in terms of ticking all the boxes, none come close to him."

Balague: "He said he learned to be an elite striker when he forgot what he had just done, like a fish. Mistakes he had in his head affected his performance. One day he just continued like it didn't matter."

Laurens: "He won the Ballon d'Or in Covid, it won't be on his mantlepiece but we all know it was his."

Fresh from his Ballon d'Or triumph, England's record goalscorer and appearance maker rides into the top three on the back of 61 goals in 51 outings for Bayern Munich last season.

Kane left the Premier League three years ago with the second-most goals ever in the competition, and is also Tottenham's all-time record scorer.

Honigstein: "Third place, at least. He is doing incredible things. At 33, still not quite at his peak. I don't think he relies on his pace, physicality, it's all about intelligence, technique. I don't think there's anyone in the game that strikes a ball as well as him right now. I don't think any player comes close that we've talked about so far in overall work-rate, defending, helping the team, making tackles in your box in the 90th minute when you're 3-0 up because he's that kind of guy. He is humble and a superb human being."

Laurens: "What I love about Kane, from a young age, a lot of this list you just knew the trajectory would be all the way to the top. Kane was not like that. It could have been very different for him. Nobody at 17 would've said he was going to win the Ballon d'Or. Nobody believed in him like the others. Kane had a very different journey."

Runner-up Thierry Henry is the only striker in the top six who is retired. A World Cup and Euros winner with France, Henry won titles with Arsenal - where he scored 228 goals - and a Champions League with Barcelona.

Honigstein: "During his time, I don't think there was a player like him in the Premier League. The way he played football was stylish, elegant, but there was almost a kind of a romantic connection between him and the ball. I don't think there was anyone near that kind of level of technique and, for want of a better word, va-va-voom he exuded. That just made for amazing viewing."

Laurens: "For me as a fellow Parisien, he's a very special player, a very special man. A career that has been incredible from a young age. It's that season in the Premier League - 24 goals, 20 assists in 38 games - not even Kane or Benzema or Cristiano, OK, Messi is different, but they haven't done that and yet he did it.

"There are still some people who undervalue him and underappreciate him and the monster that he was on the football pitch. He had it all. The pace, the style, the elegance, the technique, the mentality, the goals, the aura, the charisma. Just everything you wanted if you were to build a striker. One of the greatest football brains ever."

Of course, at number one, it's the man chasing the 1,000-goal dream. Cristiano Ronaldo may have retired from Portugal duty with 146 notches on his strike chart but the quest goes on at Al-Nassr.

The 41-year-old's evolution from tricky winger to prolific frontman has taken him from Sporting to Manchester United, twice, Real Madrid, Juventus and now Saudi Arabia.

Balague: "He's the best striker in history. His numbers are incredible. He won't stop until he gets to 1,000. He is somebody who is special. We know his background, how he wanted to be Ballon d'Or winner at 12 and didn't stop until he got there. He ran up the mountains of Funchal with weights in his ankles and snuck into the gym when it was closed to get the physique he wanted."

Laurens: "Always in the right place at the right time. The instinct. He was born with a gift, like all in this list. But you still have to work hard. Nobody will ever reach those levels again. 1,000 goals for club and country will never be done again, I think."

Honigstein: "Peak Ronaldo for me was Gareth Bale scoring and him looking very unhappy. I don't think there will be any dispute about him being the greatest. Somebody who has done it all, won it all, and he's still going for the love of it."

Man City have already secured Gianluigi Donnarumma replacement, his 92.3% and 9.33 stats are outstanding

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Man City youngster Pierce Charles can be their long-term Gianluigi Donnarumma replacement after his outstanding Nations League campaign for Northern Ireland.

Donnarumma's position as Man City's number one goalkeeper has been widely questioned following his poor start to the campaign.

The 27-year-old has only kept three clean sheets in 10 Premier League games so far and has a save percentage that ranks in the bottom half of all starting top-flight stoppers.

His indifferent showings have continued away on international duty for Italy, leading many to wonder whether he has a long-term future at the Etihad Stadium.

City need not worry, with Charles clearly on course to be their number one in the future after a breakthrough Nations League campaign as one of Northern Ireland's biggest stars.

Michael O'Neill's side completed a historic campaign with a memorable draw in Hungary on Tuesday to seal their promotion to the 'A' league.

Enzo Maresca has remained certain that Donnarumma deserves to stay in the City side, but club insiders are known to be concerned about his recent performances.

He has already made four errors leading to a goal for club and country this season, with the latest coming last week as he gave the ball away cheaply for France to score against Italy.

Sources have told Football Insider that Donnarumma could be loaned out by City if he continues to struggle, and Charles is waiting in the wings for his chance at the Etihad.

The QPR loanee ended his Nations League B campaign with just one goal conceded in six games (0.17 per 90, 93rd percentile), a 92.3 save percentage (93rd percentile) and one penalty save (100th percentile).

His distribution is maybe the most impressive part of his game, and he also boasted 0.17 assists per 90 (100th percentile), 0.33 chances created per 90 (93rd percentile) and 9.33 accurate long balls per 90 (86th percentile).

The 21-year-old is in the 100th percentile for successful passes per 90 (37.6) by goalkeepers in the Championship this season, but his save percentage has taken a hit even though QPR sit eighth as a direct result of his impressive performances.

Charles could be loaned out elsewhere in January as City aim to take advantage of his international exploits to secure a bigger club to send him on loan to.

Former Premier League stalwart and Northern Ireland international, Gareth McAuley, tipped the 21-year-old for a massive career earlier this year as he was linked with a move to Rangers.

He said: "I had Pierce with the Northern Ireland Under-19’s. He gives the game a completely different dimension. People have said to me he’s too small. But I say he’s not because he’s so agile. He’s got such quick feet that he gets around the goal. And his distribution is just on a different level.

“He’s a really top player and if Rangers could get him they’d have a massive sellable asset on their hands. If Pierce stays injury-free, I can see this kid, with his temperament, becoming a goalkeeper worth a hell of a lot of money who plays in some of the world's biggest games. He’s that good. I don’t like putting pressure on young players but he’s such a level-headed boy, it won’t faze him."

Louisville Kings begin title defense at 2027 UFL Draft. Meet the picks

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Louisville Kings' quest for back-to-back championships begins Nov. 18 in St. Louis, with the first two rounds of the 2027 United Football League Draft.

With the eighth and final pick of Round 1, coach Chris Redman & Co. selected 6-foot-8, 320-pound former Arkansas offensive tackle Dalton Wagner.

"He's got a lot of toughness, length, speed," Redman told the NFL Network. "He's going to be a huge asset for our offense. We were hoping this guy would fall to us, so really glad he did."

The Kings also hold the final pick of the second round Wednesday.

Rounds 3-15 of the UFL draft will be held Nov. 19, beginning at 8:15 a.m. on the league's official YouTube page. The draft pool includes all 2026 NFL Draft-eligible players and players from the 2023, 2024 and 2025 draft classes who were on an NFL roster on or after June 14.

As the reigning champions, the Kings will kick off the 2027 UFL season March 26 at Lynn Family Stadium. Their opponent has not yet been announced, but the game is set for an 8 p.m. kickoff and will be broadcast on Fox.

Prior to the draft, the Kings protected 35 players, including quarterback Chandler Rogers. They also retained the rights to 15 players who earned spots on NFL rosters entering the 2026 season in the event they return to the UFL in the spring. As of mid-November, only two of those players remained with teams at the sport's highest level: defensive back Bryce Hall (Houston Texans) and wide receiver Lucky Jackson (Detroit Lions).

Click on the post below to view Louisville's active roster and the players whose rights it retained:

In addition to Rogers, the most notable names still in the mix are kicker Tanner Brown, linebacker Cam Gill and running back Ian Wheeler.

Brown was named the UFL's Special Teams Player of the Year in 2026, while Gill took home its Defensive Player of the Year award. Wheeler was named the Most Valuable Player of the Kings' 27-20 victory over the DC Defenders in the United Bowl.

The Bruins and Red Wings are coming off very different results heading into Wednesday night's Atlantic Division matchup. Boston is looking to bounce back after being shut out by the Kings, while Detroit hopes to build on a much-needed victory over the Lightning.

The Bruins suffered a 3-0 loss to Los Angeles on Tuesday night, marking their first shutout of the season and extending their losing streak to three games. Michael DiPietro made 19 saves, but Boston couldn't find the back of the net.

Detroit, meanwhile, snapped a five-game losing streak with a 2-0 win over Tampa Bay on Saturday. Daniil Tarasov stopped all 32 shots he faced, while Lucas Raymond and Michael Brandsegg-Nygard scored the goals.

Karl-Anthony Towns’ ‘bittersweet’ Minnesota return comes as a Knicks champion

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

TORONTO — Karl-Anthony Towns is returning to Minnesota as an NBA champion, which solidifies the big trade as a Knicks victory but still feels "bittersweet" for the player.

"I wanted to win one there. I didn't accomplish that," Towns said ahead of Thursday's game against the Timberwolves. "But it was great to have those moments, to bring Minnesota back to the playoffs and be able to do a lot of things."

Towns spent nine seasons with the Timberwolves and served as the franchise star for most of them. There was a lot of losing but also an appearance in the conference finals, which was immediately followed by Towns' departure to New York in a 2024 blockbuster.

Although it often takes years to declare the winning side of a trade, this one can already be called in the Knicks' favor:

New York Knicks center Karl-Anthony Towns smiles after receiving his championship ring before a game on Tuesday, Oct. 20, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

●  Julius Randle, who was shipped to Minnesota for Towns, was traded in the summer to the Nets after a couple of clunky seasons with the Timberwolves. He was basically a salary dump.

●  Donte DiVincenzo, who was also sent to Minnesota for Towns, has played well when healthy but has been out since April with a torn Achilles.

●  With the 2025 first-round pick they acquired in the trade, the Timberwolves selected French center Joan Beringer at No. 17. Beringer is still only 20 years old and has appeared in just 55 games, including 15 this season.

●  Towns won a title with the Knicks, played in two All-Star Games and is playing spectacularly to start this campaign. Thursday, he comes back as a champ.

"It's going to be bittersweet," he said. "I'm just happy that I have such amazing fans and had such an amazing time in Minnesota. I love it to death. I love that place so it's going to be cool to go back."

Minnesota Timberwolves center Karl-Anthony Towns reacts after a foul call against New York Knicks forward Julius Randle on Monday, Jan. 1, 2024, in New York. Charles Wenzelberg/New York Post

The Knicks entered Toronto with a 1-6 record in close games.

They left with a 3-6 mark, and there were multiple examples of New York coming through in the clutch.

CHECK OUT THE LATEST NBA STANDINGS AND KNICKS STATS

The Knicks shot 8-for-9 on free throws (89 percent) during clutch time in the two victories over the Raptors; in their seven previous close games, the Knicks shot 6-for-10 (60 percent) from the stripe during clutch time.

Sometimes a free throw — or two — can make all the difference.

Jose Alvarado logged seven productive minutes in Tuesday's victory, which followed a string of four straight games completely out of the rotation before he played 2:22 in the first half Sunday.

Alvarado's insertion in Toronto sped up the offense. As proof, the point guard picked up four assists during the cameo.

Jose Alvarado of the New York Knicks plays against the Toronto Raptors during an NBA game in Toronto, Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. Anadolu via Getty Images

Knicks coach Mike Brown credited assistant coach Rick Brunson with the adjustment.

"Just a great call. Rick Brunson made the call of throwing Jose in to see if he can give us a spark," Brown said. "So, just a heckuva call by Rick Brunson and just a fantastic job by Jose producing on both sides of the ball when his name was called."

Towns, as the facilitator at the hub, is still producing for the Knicks. His turnovers are down over the past seven games, while the assists are up.

Brown said the center has achieved a better understanding of when to throw the home-run pass to cutters and when to accept the safer play.

"It's a fine balance because he's capable of making some beautiful passes in tight windows so you want to let him be him," Brown said. "But I thought earlier in the year — and we talked to him about it, and said it a couple times — he was trying to make the home-run pass a little too much.

"And he's gotten to a point where he's mixing it up now. … We're going to trust him because he's obviously done it for many years now with good results. We'll lean on him quite a bit in that regard but if we turn the ball over too much, we'll let him know."

Towns logged a season-high eight assists Tuesday with just two turnovers.

NFL Week 11 Injury Report: Drake Maye likely done for season, surgery for Jordan Addison

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Staying up to date on NFL injury news is of the utmost importance for fantasy football success. The article below tracks all fantasy-relevant players currently listed on their team's injury report and provides fantasy managers with actionable recommendations as new information rolls in.

Check back for updates from Tuesday through Saturday.

**UPDATED AS OF WEDNESDAY, NOVEMBER 18 AT 8:15 PM ET**

Impact: Likely none, though the Bills will employ a run-heavy approach against the Dolphins.

Palmer managed only seven offensive snaps in Week 8 before departing with an aggravation of the hamstring injury he has battled since Week 4. He was placed on injured reserve.

Impact: Not much, honestly. Palmer has played sparingly this season in a situational role, although his absence could leave room for Skyler Bell to be active for the third time this season. The Bills also activated Tyrell Shavers (knee) from the reserve/physically unable to perform list. He could join the wide receiver rotation but does not need to be rostered in 12-team leagues.

Achane suffered a torn ACL in the team's Week 3 loss to the Chiefs, ending his season.

Impact: Achane's unfortunate season-ending injury creates opportunity for Ollie Gordon and Jaylen Wright. The timing of the injury makes it likely that Achane is able to fully recover before the start of the 2027 regular season.

The Dolphins placed Willis on injured reserve in Week 10. He will be eligible to return in Week 14, but Willis is seeking a second opinion after the injury progressed into his abdomen. Season-ending surgery is possible, although the team has yet to decide if or when surgery would take place.

Impact: Kyle McCord's installation as the team's starter hurts all involved. Until we see how McCord fares in this system, only Ollie Gordon is a safe FLEX starter.

Bell is listed as doubtful for Week 10 against the Colts. He is day-to-day entering Week 11. Impact: Bell has largely been unable to get anything going during his rookie season, with the team leaning more heavily on Malik Washington and Caleb Douglas (shoulder), who was limited to begin Week 11. Bell's status could afford Kevin Coleman Jr. additional opportunities, although the low-volume Miami pass offense remains unlikely to support more than one pass catcher, now playing with a backup quarterback in Kyle McCord.

Maye is believed to have suffered a fractured ankle, an injury that would sideline him for the remainder of the 2026 season. Impact: As of Tuesday, the team continues to gather more information on the injury sustained in the first half of the Week 10 loss to the Lions. The team is also reportedly reaching out to quarterbacks as they look to right a sinking ship following consecutive losses for the first time in head coach Mike Vrabel's tenure. More to come on this developing story. Suffice to say, the fantasy value of the primary skill position players in New England takes a significant hit for the remainder of the year.

Doubs was placed on injured reserve following a shoulder injury sustained in Week 6. He will miss the next four games, at minimum.

Impact: The Patriots have now played only 33 offensive snaps with both Doubs and A.J. Brown on the field together this season after Doubs was injured on his second offensive snap in Week 6. Brown returned from injured reserve in Week 6 and should have the runway to return WR1 numbers while Doubs remains sidelined.

Brown missed Week 8 and Week 9 with ankle and knee issues before returning for Week 10 against the Lions, only to pick up a hamstring injury. The team is reportedly gathering more information during their Week 11 bye.

Impact: DeMario Douglas, Kyle Williams, Mack Hollins, and Kyle Williams led the team in snaps and routes in the absence of Brown in Week 9, with none of the bunch consistent fantasy producers. Fantasy managers are likely forced to wait until more information is released to gauge Brown's expected status in Week 12.

Henderson was placed on injured reserve ahead of Week 10 and will miss the next four games, at minimum.

Impact: Rhamondre Stevenson and Corey Kiner are likely to form a one-two punch in the absence of Henderson, with Stevenson the favorite to lead the team in opportunities and snaps. Stevenson can be viewed as a volume-backed RB2 for the time being.

Smith departed the team's Week 10 loss with an ankle injury. Follow-up testing confirmed the injury is not considered serious but his status for Week 11 against the Chiefs is in doubt. He was officially limited on Wednesday.

Impact: The fantasy upside of all primary skill position players for the Jets would take a significant hit should Smith be unavailable against the Chiefs. Fantasy managers will want to monitor this situation closely in the coming days.

Cooper aggravated the same ankle injury he has been dealing with for multiple weeks in Week 10, placing his status in doubt for Week 11. He was sidelined to begin the week.

Impact: Target volume would likely concentrate further on Garrett Wilson and Adonai Mitchell should Cooper be unable to play against the Chiefs. Isaiah Williams (ribs) is also hurt.

Jackson returned from an ankle injury in Week 8 and made it all of one offensive snap before suffering a thumb injury that is likely to sideline him for the remainder of the regular season.

Impact: Jackson's absence is the biggest hit to the fantasy value of alpha receiver Zay Flowers, who falls to a mid-range WR2 in rest of season rankings. Recent signee Jacoby Brissett is expected to start under center in Week 10.

Huntley suffered a season-ending turf toe injury in the team's Week 8 loss to the Bills.

Smythe suffered a torn Achilles in the team's Week 4 win over the Titans. His season is done.

Impact: It was Matt Hibner who saw the largest increase in snaps following Smythe's departure in Week 4, making it unlikely we see veteran Mark Andrews in a more robust role.

The Bengals placed Iosivas on injured reserve in Week 3. The team activated his 21-day practice window this week but he is listed as doubtful to play in Week 10 against the Steelers.

Impact: Wide receivers Colbie Young and Dohnte Meyers will duke it out for the No. 3 wide receiver role for at least the next four weeks. Meyers can be added in 14-team leagues, though his upside is questionable.

All suffered a torn ACL in Week 7. He was placed on injured reserve Tuesday and will miss the remainder of the regular season.

Impact: All loses yet another season in devastating fashion after suffering his third torn ACL. His absence could provide a slight uptick in snap rate and route participation for Mike Gesicki, although he will be a difficult weekly start for as long as both Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins remain healthy.

Impact: He will be eligible to return after four games missed.

Miami Heat go all out with Vice City night ahead of 'Grand Theft Auto 6' release

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

MIAMI — In this corner of Vice City, at least it was dry.

Outside, torrential downpours that lasted all day and had threatened flooding throughout South Florida didn't deter thousands of fans from lining up outside the Kaseya Center for a Miami Heat-Milwaukee Bucks game that was rebranded to celebrate the midnight release of "Grand Theft Auto 6." The game is set in a fictional city named Leonida, otherwise known as Vice City, that is very closely based on Miami.

The video game franchise has a massive fan base and this one has been in production for more than a decade, leading to intense enthusiasm and anticipation for the latest release. Fans, donned in ponchos and raincoats being blasted by the wind, were lined up outside the Kaseya Center more than four hours before tipoff.

Inside, the vibe was euphoric and festive, and the interest in the activations was overwhelming. A custom court with electric pink and blue paint matched the pink at center court, where "Vice City" was painted in the video game's font.

Vice City-branded apparel was sold at several stores across the arena, and lines to get in to purchase items wrapped around more than half of the bowl. One fan said he had been in line for around two hours; he was still several hundred patrons away.

A store selling jerseys, the Heat Jersey Lab, was stocked only with special edition Vice City jerseys to commemorate the activation. With about 45 minutes to go until tipoff, some of the slots where jerseys had hung had been cleared out.

"This is a really unique night," Heat coach Erik Spoelstra told reporters before the game. "It's going to be a lot of fun. It's going to be a global game tonight because of the setup with GTA. I think it's awesome. I think it's fun. It's 10x to everything."

The activations were consistent throughout the night. Fast-food restaurants had been rebranded into ones made popular in the video game franchise; in sections 105, 120, 308 and 324 were "Burger Shots" and in section 325 was a "Cluckin' Bell," a fried chicken joint.

During stoppages in play, the jumbotrons above the floor played advertisements that promoted fictional businesses from the video game; late in the first quarter, one for "Status Auto Imports" was played, with the footage all being gameplay from Grand Theft Auto.

Even the Heat's public address announcer, Gale Nelson, introduced the team to fans as "your Vice City Heat." Later, throughout the night, entertainers did not mention Miami whatsoever, instead referring to the home city by the video game moniker.

"It's interesting," Bucks coach Taylor Jenkins told reporters before the game. "I'm not one to play Gand Theft Auto, never was. I was more of a sports video game guy. But anytime you can cross collaborate with sports and entertainment, I think it's a great opportunity to generate fandom for all parties involved.

"Driving to the arena, even with all the rain coming down, seeing all the people waiting outside, there's going to be a lot of electricity in here."

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Miami Heat go all out with Vice City night ahead of 'Grand Theft Auto 6' release

New GTA 6 Players, Be Careful When Claiming Your GTA+ Pre-Order Bonus

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

All GTA 6 pre-orders come with a free month of GTA+, which nets you some in-game bonuses like a free vehicle, garage, and higher selling prices.

But before you excitedly claim your freebie, it’s essential you understand auto-renew will be enabled.

This means unless you disable it, you’ll be charged the monthly price of $7.99/£6.99/€7.99 as soon as your free month is over.

Once you’ve claimed your free trial, from the PS5’s home screen scroll left to the PS Store and select ‘...’ > Subscriptions:

You’ll see all of your active subscriptions listed.

Select ‘GTA+’ and press ‘Cancel Subscription’:

Don’t worry, you’ll still get to enjoy the entirety of your free month, this will just prevent you from being charged.

If you want to keep GTA+, then of course you don’t need to do this. Just be aware you will get charged every month. You can also re-activate your subscription if you choose to do so by repeating the steps above:

To be fair, the PS5 does tell you about this when activating your free month, but it’s GTA 6 release day and I suspect some of you will be giddy and not necessarily in the mood for the fine print.

Yaxel Lendeborg shares shocking details in viral bench moment with Draymond Green

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Warriors rookie sensation Yaxel Lendeborg was seen visibly upset during Golden State's blowout loss to the Pelicans on Monday night — forcing 15-year veteran Draymond Green to come comfort him.

Before the Warriors take the court on the road against the Mavericks on Wednesday night, Lendeborg spoke about the interaction between him and Green and revealed what advice he was given.

Draymond Green on the court during Monday's loss to the Pelicans. NBAE via Getty Images

"He basically told me to stop showing so much weakness," Lendeborg said. "If he's on the other team, he's gonna think that I'm weak and go at me. I want someone to go at me anyway, but the way I was showing it off, it's kinda like I'm soft. I've just gotta fix my mental."

Lendeborg admitted that while he's early in his NBA career, he's going through some adversity.

Yaxel Lendeborg seen visibly frustrated during Monday's loss to the Pelicans. Getty Images

The Warriors were riding the high of an eight-game winning streak until Steph Curry went down with an oblique injury on Nov. 11. Now, they're on a three-game losing streak and have put Lendeborg in unfamiliar territory.

"I hate losing, man. Last year, I lost a total of three games. The year before that, I can't even remember how much," Lendeborg said. "But I've never lost this much so early. So me putting my head down like that, I felt like I let the team down."

While Lendeborg has high standards, the Warriors are asking him to grow up quicker than expected in Curry's absence.

In the New Orleans loss, Lendeborg was Golden State's leading scorer, finishing with 28 points. He added eight rebounds and three assists.

The Warriors' Yaxel Lendeborg looks to get past a defender in Monday night's game against the Pelicans. AP Photo/Morgan Simmons

But even with his solid performance, the Warriors dug themselves into an early hole and got outscored 35-11 in the first quarter.

Golden State coach Steve Kerr said after the game the team had no "juice" in the first half and is contemplating changes to its starting five.

Giannis Antetokounmpo, Heat host Tyler Herro, Bucks for GTA 6-inspired 'Vice City' night

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Miami Heat are playing host to the Milwaukee Bucks on Wednesday night. While this was already a big game — the first time Giannis Antetokounmpo is facing his former team — it carries even more meaning.

The long-awaited "GTA VI" releases nationwide on Thursday. It is arguably the most highly anticipated video game of all time, coming out 13 years after GTA V was released back in 2013.

The game, which has already received near-perfect reviews, is set to upend the world Thursda. With estimates that roughly 16 million copies of the game were pre-ordered, employers could be dealing with a lot of "sick" days ahead of the weekend.

That might sound far-fetched, but it's true. Experts are estimating businesses in the United States could lose over $1 billion in productivity due to worker absences.

"Vice City" is a fictional city from the franchise. It is not a direct replica of Miami, but it is heavily inspired by the city, while the state of "Leonida" in the video game is inspired by Florida.

The Heat's marketing department took the chance to host a "Vice City"-inspired night in honor of the game's release, and it just so happened to coincide with Anteokounmpo getting his first crack at his old team.

To celebrate the game's release, the Kaseya Center got a "Vice City" makeover with a new paint job. Music from the video game was also played throughout the arena.

The game is also expected to feature a unique halftime show and a drone show for fans' entertainment. The arena even brought some of the fictional fast-food joints from the video game to the concession options for Wednesday night.

ESPN reporter Cam Wolf showed that fans lined up four hours before tipoff in the rain to get access to the "Vice City"-inspired gear that was selling in the team store.

Wolfe also added that the arena completely sold out of the gear just 90 minutes after it went on sale.

ESPN released a video package for the game.

Milwaukee traded Antetokounmpo and Bobby Portis to Miami in exchange for Tyler Herro, Jaime Jaquez Jr., Kel'el Ware, Kasparas Jakučionis, and five total draft picks on July 6, 2026.

Antetokounmpo has already made an impact with his new team. Heading into Wednesday's game, he has played in 14 games this year and is averaging 28.3 points, 12.5 rebounds, and 5.2 assists per game.

The Heat are 8-6, while the Bucks enter with an NBA-worst 1-13 record.

Ranking Fifa: Spagna al comando. Spicca il Marocco. Balzo storico del Canada. L'Italia...

L'Italia targata Mancini 2.0 guadagna una posizione nel ranking Fifa, aggiornato dopo l'intensa finestra internazionale di novembre, e sale dal 15/o al 14/ posto. Le prime quattro posizioni rimangono invariate: la Spagna, campione del mondo, mantiene il comando, seguita da Argentina, Francia e Inghilterra. Getty Images

Nelle posizioni di vertice, spicca il Marocco che sale al quinto posto, sua miglior posizione di sempre, superando il Portogallo (6/o) e il Brasile (7/o), che scivolano entrambi di una posizione. Belgio e Olanda restano ottava e nona e nella top 10 rientra la Colombia, per la prima volta dall'ottobre 2024, a spese del Messico, che perde tre posizioni, finendo alle spalle di Germania (11/a, +2) e Svizzera (12/a, -) e subito davanti agli azzurri. Nella top 50, crescono Canada (25/o, +2), Corea del Sud (30/a, +2), Paraguay (31/o, +2), Mali (43/o, +4) e Slovenia (49/a, +3). L'Uefa esprime la metà dei membri della top 50, la Caf ne conta dieci, la Conmebol sette, mentre AFC e Concacaf ne hanno quattro ciascuna.

BIG stacks timber volumes with alpine views for new neighborhood in switzerland

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

bjarke ingels group proposes four timber buildings in thun, switzerland with stepped hotel terraces overlooking the alps.

Bjarke Ingels Group / BIG unveils plans for four timber buildings in Thun, Switzerland, where the architecture firm will help shape the entrance to the city’s emerging Siegenthalergut district. The 13,000-square-meter development brings together two hotels and residential buildings, with stacked wooden volumes that project outward above the street. Set against the Bernese Alps, the architecture is defined by deep window grids and large terraces carved into the buildings’ stepped facades.

The proposal, developed with landscape architects August + Margrith Künzel, was selected through a competition organized by Frutiger AG and the city of Thun. Titled Nachwachsende Stadt, or Growing City, the scheme occupies one of the first two plots planned for the new neighborhood. The site sits opposite a sports center and the future headquarters of the Swiss Football Association to give the BIG-designed buildings a prominent position along the city’s western entrance.

At the southwest corner of the site, the team at BIG brings a three-star and a four-star hotel together under one roof. Shared amenity spaces cut diagonally through the structure, creating a series of setbacks that interrupt its otherwise regular timber grid. These shifts make room for outdoor terraces overlooking the neighboring sports facilities, with the Bernese Alps rising in the distance. A restaurant at the top of the building will give guests an even broader view of the surrounding landscape.

At street level, a cantilevered corner marks the main hotel entrance, opening onto a drop-off plaza beneath the projecting upper floors. Commercial spaces line the adjacent boulevard, bringing activity to the building’s ground floor. Across the facade, large square windows sit within deeply framed timber modules, while recessed portions reveal warmer, sheltered spaces behind the exterior grid. The alternating volumes give the hotel a sculptural profile that changes depending on where it is viewed from.

To the east, the remaining timber buildings accommodate residences with flexible layouts and projecting bays. Here, the same geometric language takes on a more domestic scale. Balconies extend outward from the facades, while recessed loggias create sheltered outdoor rooms. The shifting arrangement of these openings gives each elevation a different rhythm, with the projecting volumes bringing daylight deeper into the apartments.

The residential buildings face an internal courtyard, where winding paths move through gardens and planted gathering spaces. Renderings picture grassy areas beneath the timber facades, with balconies overlooking the shared landscape. Small plazas and seating areas are distributed throughout the development, connecting the homes to the surrounding neighborhood.

Of the project’s total 13,000 square meters, approximately 6,500 are dedicated to residential uses, alongside 6,000 square meters of hotel space and another 400 square meters for commercial activities.

The use of timber extends across all four buildings, establishing a consistent material language while helping reduce the development’s embodied carbon footprint. BIG has designed the timber elements for future disassembly and reuse, allowing components to be recovered as the buildings change over time. The deep balconies and roof overhangs also serve a practical purpose, providing natural shade in summer and shelter during the colder months.

Green roofs and rainwater retention systems will help manage water across the site, while approximately 1,800 square meters of photovoltaic panels are planned to generate renewable energy. The development is targeting Switzerland’s SNBS Gold sustainability certification.

The winning proposal will now progress through further design and approval stages, with construction of the district’s first buildings possible from 2028. For BIG, which opened its Zurich office in 2023, the project adds another timber-focused development to its growing Swiss portfolio, alongside the planned Dock A terminal at Zurich Airport.

Chiefs’ Travis Kelce set to ride off into sunset soon, so who could replace him in 2027 NFL Draft?

Chiefs' Travis Kelce set to ride off into sunset soon, so who could replace him in 2027 NFL Draft? appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Kansas City Chiefs may soon face the difficult task of finding a successor to Travis Kelce, and the 2027 NFL Draft could offer an answer. With the veteran tight end nearing the later stages of his career, Kansas City must consider how to fill the role he has held in the offense.

ESPN's latest 2027 NFL mock draft identifies Oregon tight end Jamari Johnson as a possible replacement. Analyst Field Yates projects the Chiefs to select Johnson with the 24th overall pick, describing him as a "high-potential heir apparent" to Kelce. The projection comes as uncertainty grows around Kansas City's tight end position beyond the 2026 season.

Kelce, 37, could be playing his final NFL season. His eventual departure would leave a significant void for quarterback Patrick Mahomes and the Chiefs' offense. The situation is further complicated by Noah Gray and Jared Wiley, who are both entering the final years of their contracts in 2027.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Johnson has strengthened his draft case with his production at Oregon. He is averaging 50.6 receiving yards per game this season, the 10th-highest mark among FBS tight ends. Yates noted that Johnson has maintained that output despite Oregon's quarterback issues. The prospect also stands out for his controlled tempo, decisiveness and ability to recognize space.

The mock draft remains an early projection, and Kansas City's needs could change before April. Still, Jamari Johnson is a name worth monitoring as the Chiefs plan for life after Travis Kelce. Replacing a future Hall of Famer will not be simple, but the 2027 draft could give the franchise a first step.

Le Tissier Tipped Him As Southampton's Next Lallana, Now He Plays Non-League Football

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

In the last 50 years, Southampton are widely recognised as having one of the best academies in football.

From bringing through the likes of Matthew Le Tissier and Alan Shearer in the 1980s, to Alex Oxlade-Chamberlain and James Ward-Prowse in more recent years, Southampton have made a lot of money through player sales.

As well as Le Tissier and Shearer, considered to be two of the greatest Premier League players of all time, Gareth Bale and Theo Walcott are two more players from the academy who went on to play at the highest level for Real Madrid and Arsenal.

Luke Shaw was another who came through the ranks before joining Manchester United, where he still is 13 years later, and Calum Chambers was another who left St Mary's for Arsenal after breaking through under Mauricio Pochettino.

It's fair to say that those success stories have dried up a little in more recent years, while the club has spent more time battling at the bottom of the Premier League and in the Championship, with a number of players failing to make the grade having made first-team appearances.

Striker Sam Gallagher left for Blackburn Rovers in 2019, Matt Targett joined Aston Villa after a 15-year association with the south coast outfit, while Harrison Reed signed for Fulham after failing to break into the midfield at St Mary's.

Two of the more recent rare success stories are Tyler Dibling, who joined Everton for a huge fee in the summer of 2025, and Jimmy-Jay Morgan, who is currently scoring for fun at West Bromwich Albion after leaving Saints for Chelsea as a 17-year-old.

Southampton have had other young players who looked on course for a big future at St Mary's too, especially following their U21 Premier League Cup win against Blackburn in 2015.

After watching the final live, Saints legend Le Tissier was asked by Sky Sports to pick out five youngsters to watch who had first-team potential, and he selected Gallagher, Targett, Reed and Sam McQueen.

Le Tissier was particularly complimentary about his final pick, Jake Hesketh, who joined Saints' academy at the age of 10 having been born in Stockport.

He reminds me of Adam Lallana in the way he plays. He's got good feet, moves with the ball really well, and is very, very natural.

"However, he's got a bit of growing and strengthening to do and it won't be an instant thing for him to break through to senior level."

It turned out that Le Tissier may have been right, about his warning over Hesketh.

The teenager was actually promoted to the first-team by Ronald Koeman during the 2014-15 season because of injury issues, and he made his debut for the club in a 2-1 home defeat to Manchester United in December.

Hesketh made a further top flight appearance against Burnley, but he then fell back down the pecking order until Claude Puel was appointed as Koeman's successor in 2016.

He made two further appearances under the Frenchman, scoring his first senior goal in a League Cup tie against Crystal Palace in September 2016, and he also played in the Europa League tie away against Hapoel Be'er Sheva.

The attacking midfielder then picked up an injury and even though he signed a new four-and-a-half year contract in December 2016, he never wore the Southampton shirt again.

He spent time on loan at Burton Albion, MK Dons, Lincoln City and Crawley Town, before leaving St Mary's on a permanent basis for Eastleigh in 2021 after 17 years with the club.

Following two seasons with the National League outfit, Hesketh remained in the Southampton area when he joined Southern League Premier Division club Sholing FC, where he still plays to this day.as a 30-year-old.

The former Southampton star was on the wrong side of the law after leaving St Mary's

Is Mauricio Pochettino’s job at Manchester United the toughest in the Premier League?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United hired Mauricio Pochettino as their next manager to replace Michael Carrick, who managed just one win in 10 games.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

As he faces Liverpool for his first game on Sunday, the question arises: is he facing the toughest job in the Premier League?

The question was posed on BBC Radio about who is going to have more pressure, Pochettino or Thomas Frank at Crystal Palace.

According to former Premier League defender Nedum Onuoha, the expectations surrounding Manchester United will make the Argentine’s job considerably harder.

Pochettino is preparing for his first game in charge of Manchester United, with Liverpool waiting at Anfield on November 22. However, the challenge facing the new manager goes beyond getting results on the pitch.

Onuoha believes Pochettino will have to deal with constant discussion and criticism about his work, much of it coming from former United players who are now in the media.

Choosing between Pochettino and Frank, Onuoha said, “Probably Pochettino, because every former Manchester United player works in the media, so there will always be discussion around what he is doing.

“Whereas Frank can just enjoy playing his football at Crystal Palace. If he does well then it is great, if he doesn’t do well then it is still OK.

“But from Pochettino’s perspective, if Manchester United finish the season 15th in the Premier League, whether we like it or not, there will be some criticism of him because he’s had 28 games to do something.”

The comments just highlight the difference in expectations at the two clubs. Frank may have more freedom to work without the same level of attention, while Pochettino will be judged from day one against the standards historically associated with Manchester United. That is one big problem for Pochettino.

Rory Smith from The Observer highlighted another glaring problem at United.  Highlighting a major problem, Smith said, “there is a cycle element to Manchester United whereby a manager comes in, makes things a bit better, and then within a year to 18 months they aren’t challenging for titles again because the Premier League has changed since they were a dominant force.”

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22. That opening fixture will give him an immediate test, but the bigger task will be to get back United to winning ways, step by step.

Will the Manchester United job be without pressure for Mauricio Pochettino? Absolutely Not.

It is belived that the La Liga club are so desperate to let the defender leave - so they can free up space in their wage budget for new signings - that they could agree to a deal that would favour United considerably.

It’s Tay’s first time at the show in 13 years!

The 2026 CMA Awards are taking place in Nashville tonight, and Swifties can barely contain their excitement over Taylor Swift’s big return to her country roots. This marks the megastar’s first time at the show in 13 years, so…it’s a pretty big deal.

Aside from the T. Swift of it all, it’s sure to be a huge night for other stars like Ella Langley, Megan Moroney, and more. Ahead of Wednesday night’s festivities, host Lainey Wilson leaked part of the CMA seating chart in a hilarious video posted to her social channels on November 17.

Based on this seating chart, I, too, will be sat for Taylor’s big comeback performance at the CMAs tonight!

Sheffield Wednesday star Barry Bannan posts hilarious Aston Villa throwback

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sheffield Wednesday icon Barry Bannan is closing in on 500 appearances for the Owls, having returned to the club in the summer. However, he's not always been at Hillsborough and was actually quite the journeyman at the start of his career.

It was at Aston Villa where it all started for the Scottish international, having taken on an opportunity with the club after a couple of years with Celtic's youth academy.

Bannan's debut came in December 2008 in a UEFA Cup clash with Hamburg - a game that saw Villa lose 3-1. That was around the time that the 36-year-old had signed his first professional contract with the club, a moment that he's been reflecting on.

Over on Instagram, Bannan is particularly active on his story, sharing photos and comments.

On Wednesday morning, he shared an image of the day he signed his first professional contract with Villa, around 2008, surrounded by what looks like family, a potential agent and/or club officials.

Bannan can quite clearly see the funny side of the image that was taken 18 years ago, with a laughing emoji accompanying the words: "First ever pro contract at Aston Villa flashback".

The midfielder would play 82 times for Villa in all competitions, scoring two goals. In the period that he was at Villa Park, he had loans with Derby County, Blackpool and Leeds United, before sealing a move to Crystal Palace on summer transfer deadline day 2013.

Bannan was a tidy footballer at Villa and highly-rated young midfielder. His time at Palace brought a loan to Bolton and, ultimately, wasn't the success that the South London club had hoped.

By the time August 2015 came, Bannan was signing with Sheffield Wednesday and tweeting: "Glad to have joined @swfc on a permanent transfer today new start and glad to be going somewhere to play regular football and a fresh start."

Barry Bannan stats at Sheffield Wednesday (18/11/2026)

He got that regular football as Sheffield Wednesday challenged for promotion to the Premier League in his first two years at the club. The Scotland international became a real cult hero in that time, and has subsequently stuck by Wednesday through a lot of on-field and off-field turmoil.

Bannan is now approaching 500 games for the Owls, taking only a short break from representing the club at the start of 2026 when he joined promotion-chasing Millwall at a time Wednesday's off-field turmoil at the hands of Dejphon Chansiri was at its worst.

After returning in the summer, Bannan is eyeing another League One promotion with Sheffield Wednesday - the club that's been his first proper home since he agreed those professional terms with Villa 18 years ago.

Manchester United are reportedly prepared to set aside funds to sign Monaco striker Folarin Balogun during the January transfer window, with Mauricio Pochettino keen to reunite with the American international at Old Trafford.

The Red Devils are expected to strengthen their squad in several positions when the transfer window reopens, and adding more quality to their attack could also be part of their plans.

According to a Football Insider report, Manchester United are prioritising defensive reinforcements before considering a move for Balogun. However, the club could reserve some money for the striker if their budget allows after addressing their defensive shortcomings.

The 25-year-old has emerged as an interesting option for Pochettino, who previously worked with him while he was in charge of the United States national team.

Balogun reportedly has a strong relationship with the Argentine manager, and that connection could help convince him to move to Manchester.

Monaco are also prepared to sanction his departure in January as they look to generate funds for their own recruitment plans. The striker came close to joining Everton during the summer, but the proposed transfer collapsed after medical concerns emerged.

The former Arsenal academy graduate has nevertheless demonstrated his quality in French football, scoring 19 goals across all competitions for Monaco last season.

Manchester United could benefit from adding another reliable goalscorer to their squad, particularly with Benjamin Sesko currently struggling with injury problems.

Balogun has experience playing in English football and could adapt quickly to the demands of the Premier League.

However, United’s financial priorities mean that a move is far from guaranteed. Much will depend on how much money remains available after their defensive recruitment.

Nevertheless, Pochettino’s interest and Monaco’s willingness to sell could give the Red Devils an opportunity to negotiate a favourable deal in January.
